# CHECK-DEBT — the ledger of owed-but-unwritten checks

**Home mandated by `VERIFY-AND-CHECKS.md` A.4.** Opened ARC 010 (2026-08-10).

Every environment change owes a check (`nix_check_contract.md` §1). The check system is built
late by deliberate sequencing (doctrine A.7), so that obligation is **recorded here rather than
blocking the arc**.

**Do not build a gate on "this ledger is empty."** Doctrine A.7 records the measured
counter-example: on the predecessor system this series rose monotonically — 95 → ~190 across
seventeen arcs — and never once fell. The target is per-arc movement, not zero.

**A debt names the arc that can actually discharge it, or names nobody.** Doctrine B.3: *an owner
that cannot pay is no owner wearing a name.* `owner: unassigned` is honest; an owner pointed at an
arc whose own scope forbids the fix is furniture.

## RULE OF RECORD — BINDING. A compensating control must be AIMED before it is checked for existence.

**Ratified ARC 023 (Phase 4) out of sub-agent B's compensating-control sweep. Recorded here as a
standing rule because it changes what a row may offer as its reason for accepting a residual.**

> When a debt row accepts a residual on the strength of a **compensating control**, two questions
> must be answered in order, and the second one is the one this ledger kept skipping:
>
> 1. **Does the control exist and does it measure anything?** D3.15's subject — `check_datafeed_bar_seal`
>    arm 4 reported `not synthesisable from annotations` for all five published types and measured
>    nothing, while two rows cited it as their compensating control.
> 2. **Is the control AIMED at this row's residual?** D2.21's subject is guard **polarity in the
>    source**; arm 4 constructs published **types** and drives immutability on them. An inverted guard
>    in `_ingest_history` is on no path arm 4 executes. **Even fully repaired, arm 4 could never have
>    covered that residual** — and ARC 023 confirmed this after repairing it, which is the measurement
>    that makes this a rule rather than an opinion.
>
> **A control that measures the wrong thing is not weaker evidence than one that measures nothing —
> it is the same evidence wearing a better disguise**, because question 1 returns a reassuring answer.
> Aim is therefore checked FIRST from here on.
>
> Corollary, measured in the same arc: the reverse case is real and must not be assumed away. D2.20's
> named control **is** aimed — arm B1 reads no names at all, so any derivation from the request reports
> a mode where the sentinel is owed. That row stays open and narrowed on a control that both measures
> and aims. **The rule disqualifies mis-aimed controls; it does not disqualify controls.**

---

## RULE OF RECORD — BINDING. What a can-fail against a fake does and does not prove.

**Ratified ARC 022 (C1) out of debt row D3.10. Recorded here as a standing rule of this ledger,
not merely as a debt, because it changes what every future row may claim.**

> A gate's can-fail against a **purpose-built fake** proves the gate *can* discriminate, not that it
> discriminates **against its real subject**. Until a can-fail is demonstrated against the real
> subject the gate governs, the gate is recorded **UNBOUND**, and the debt it covers is **not
> narrowed**.
>
> A gate binds **per subject, not once.** A gate bound against one adapter is unbound against the
> next, because the next presents a third shape.

**This is ratified because ARC 021 MEASURED it, not because it reads well.** Both new datafeed gates
had six plants between them, every plant failing and naming its site, every control restored
byte-identical — all against fakes. Phase 4 then planted **two real D1.13 defects in the real
adapter and BOTH GATES PASSED, and so did all 49 of the adapter's own tests.** Six fake-based plants
had all failed correctly; two real ones did not. The evidence is in D3.9 and D3.10 below.

**What the rule obliges, mechanically:**

1. A row may not be narrowed or discharged on the strength of a fake-based can-fail. `NARROWED` and
   `discharged ARC <n>` both require the plant to have landed in the artefact the gate governs — in
   a **scratch copy** of the tree (doctrine C.8 / `debug.md` §7.2), which is the venue, never the
   subject. Real subject, reversible venue. ARC 020's plant of a genuine 3× retry into
   `IBKRBrokerOrder._emit_flatten_leg` is the worked example of the correct form; ARC 021's planted
   adapter is the worked example of the form that does not bind.
2. **A second subject re-opens the question.** When a gate's scope grows a module, the gate is
   UNBOUND against that module until a plant lands in it, whatever its record against the first.
3. **UNBOUND is a verdict, not an accusation.** Several gates below are UNBOUND for reasons that are
   good — their subject is the hardware's volume UUID, or the system interpreter, or a `.git/hooks`
   directory shared by every linked worktree, and breaking it to prove a point would cost more than
   the proof is worth. The rule does not ask those gates to be broken. It asks the ledger to stop
   reading their green as binding.

**The census taken under this rule is ARC 022 (C2)** — every registered gate, BOUND or UNBOUND,
each with its arc and its evidence. It is the block immediately below the series table.

Discovery is now driven from the SETTLED ROSTER (the `DATAFEED_PORT_VERBS` verb the port Protocol declares as returning `MarketDataMode`), the drive is a subscribe/grant/re-subscribe LIFECYCLE on a constructed subject rather than a call with an integer, and **non-vacuity is asserted on every run by `sys.settrace` rather than by a docstring**: the gate reports which functions of the subject's own file executed and refuses to PASS unless the mode verb is among them. **THE ARC'S HEADLINE IS A POSITIVE RESULT, WHICH THIS SERIES HAS NOT HAD FROM THIS SUBJECT BEFORE.** ARC 022 measured exit 2 on control and exit 2 on both ARC 021 real plants — identical verdicts, no discrimination. ## THE BINDING CENSUS — ARC 022 (C2)

**Every registered gate, against the rule of record above. The gate list is DERIVED, never typed:**
`checks/registry.json`'s four blocks union'd, cross-checked against `ls checks/check_*.py`. **The two
agree exactly at 12** — which is not an incidental convenience, it is the standing claim
`registered_check_count` (`derived:checks_glob=12, derived:registry_json=12`) being true on the day
the census was taken. Had they disagreed, the census would have covered the union and said so.

**Nine of the twelve verdicts rest on evidence banked by an earlier arc. Five of those nine were
RE-TAKEN in ARC 022 rather than read** — the brief's instruction was *confirm rather than assume*,
and the two datafeed gates are the standing proof that a banked can-fail can be true and still not
bind. Each re-take is a four-output cycle in a **throwaway copy of the tree**, `__pycache__` purged
between every step, sha256 on both sides of the plant. No plant touched this worktree.

| gate | verdict | evidence, and how it was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| `check_python_runtime` | **UNBOUND** | No can-fail exists, in any arc. `sessions/SESSION.md` has **zero** occurrences of the gate's name. Its only negative case monkeypatches the gate's own threshold constant (`scripts/tests/test_check_python_runtime.py:34`, `monkeypatch.setattr(mod, "MINIMUM", (99, 0))`) — that moves the ruler, not the thing measured, and cannot separate *detects an old interpreter* from *compares two integers*. Row **D3.11** |
| `check_venv` | **UNBOUND** | Every `check_venv` hit in `sessions/SESSION.md` is the FILE being used as bait for a **hook's** can-fail (D3.1 ARC 010 at `:439`, D3.4 ARC 018 at `:1184`, D3.5 at `:1190`), never the gate under test. Its own negatives drive empty `tmp_path` homes and a monkeypatched `sys.prefix`. Confirmed in ARC 022 that binding it is **refused on purpose**: this worktree's `.venv` is a symlink to `/home/bbt/nix/.venv`, shared by the parent and both sibling agents, so breaking it to prove a point would disarm two other agents mid-arc — the exact hazard `check_hook_suite` was built around. Row **D3.12** |
| `check_node_identity` | **UNBOUND** | Negatives are a fabricated UUID constant against a `tmp_path` state file (`scripts/tests/test_check_node_identity.py:45-48`, `UUID_B = "00000000-1111-…"`). The real-subject test is labelled a control in its own docstring (`:82`, *"this box's … is known-good … so the real outcome must be PASS"*). The subject is this hardware's volume UUID and cannot be perturbed without replacing the disk. Row **D3.13** |
| `check_python_deps` | **BOUND** | ARC 008 banked the four-output cycle against the real `checks/pinned_deps.json`. **RE-TAKEN ARC 022 rather than read**: CONTROL `pass: pins satisfied: ib_async==2.1.0, pytest-asyncio==1.4.0` exit 0 (sha256 `db23631d…`) → PLANT `2.1.0` → `2.0.1` (sha256 `e63b859b…`) → `fail_repairable: ib_async: 2.1.0 (want 2.0.1)` exit 1 → RESTORE sha256 back to `db23631d…` → exit 0. ## THE COMPENSATING-CONTROL SWEEP — ARC 023 (B2)

**D3.15 generalised.** That row records one instance: `check_datafeed_bar_seal` arm 4 was named by
**both** D2.20 and D2.21 as the compensating control for a structural residual, and it measures
nothing. The obvious question is how many other rows are standing on a control nobody has run.

**THE ROW SET IS DERIVED, NOT LISTED.** A regex over every `D1./D2./D3.` row for the vocabulary
this ledger actually uses when one row leans on another instrument — `compensating control`,
`mitigat`, `corroborat`, `covered by`, `canary`, `the immediate hole is closed by`, `what actually
fails that plant` — returned **11 of 79 rows**. Every one was then read and classified by whether
the row's ACCEPTANCE rests on the named control, which is the architect's own predicate and is
narrower than the vocabulary. Four of the eleven do not qualify and saying why is part of the
result, because a sweep that quietly drops its non-hits is a hand list wearing a regex.

| row | the named control | does it actually measure? | disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D2.20** | *"Arm B1's behavioural drive is the compensating control"* | **NO — HALF VACUOUS, RE-MEASURED TODAY.** Arm B1 is D3.16's subject. Run on this tree it returns `arm B1 could not DRIVE its subject — broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:granted_mode(0\|1\|3): AttributeError: 'int' object has no attribute '_symbols'` on all three legs, and the gate is CANNOT_MEASURE. The behavioural drive named here **has never executed the adapter's accessor**. The row's SECOND control — *"the pytest pair added in Phase 4 is what actually fails that plant"* — **does** measure: `test_resubscribe_rearms_the_sentinel_rather_than_inheriting_the_old_grant` and `test_adapter_wide_granted_mode_reports_the_grant_never_the_request` both exist in `scripts/tests/test_broker_datafeed.py`, both drive the real adapter, both pass | already OPEN, so there is no severity to restore. Recorded in the row |
| **D2.21** | *"Arm 4's behavioural drive is the compensating control"* | **NO, AND WORSE THAN VACUOUS — IT IS MIS-TARGETED.** Two independent reasons, and the second is new: (1) arm 4 measures nothing at all (D3.15, re-measured today — all five published types `not synthesisable from annotations`); (2) **even a working arm 4 could not have covered this residual.** D2.21's subject is guard POLARITY in the SOURCE (`if found is not None: store[k]=v`). Arm 4 constructs published TYPES and drives immutability on them. An inverted guard in `_ingest_history` is not on any path arm 4 executes, so the control was never competent for the property it was offered for | **NOT REOPENED, and the refusal is argued rather than asserted — see below** |
| **D2.16** | *"covered by all eight hooks … and by `scripts/tests/test_runtime_gate.py`"* | **PARTLY — MEASURED 7 OF 8, NOT 8.** pre-commit's own `Classifier` over `git.get_all_files()` selects `scripts/runtime_gate.py` for `ruff-check`, `ruff-format`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit (production)`, `complexipy` and `pytest-affected` (`always_run`) — **`bandit (tests)` does not**, because its `files: ^scripts/tests/` excludes it. The row's own parenthetical names SEVEN hooks while its prose says eight, so it contradicts itself in the same sentence. The companion test exists and its first assertion does derive the invoked path out of `.pre-commit-config.yaml` | acceptance stands (the substantive control is real); the count is **corrected in the row**. **This is D3.1's defect verbatim, eight arcs later**: one claim recorded against two bandit instruments |
| **D2.19** | *"the only mitigation is that every selected row id is printed on every run"* | **YES — RE-EXECUTED.** Both depth probes print their full selection to stderr on every run: the order side prints `selected: D1.17, D1.19, …, D3.8` and its vocabulary, the datafeed side likewise. The mitigation is weak by design and it is real | unchanged |
| **D3.10** | *"The immediate hole is closed by two pytest cases added in Phase 4"* | **YES.** Both named tests are on disk, drive the real adapter, and carry the plant they were written against in their own docstrings | unchanged |
| **D3.14** | *"the throwaway repo carries the **real** pre-commit configuration and resolves the **real** pinned store environments"* | **YES**, and ARC 023 (B) went further and bound arms 1 and 2 against the real repository — see that row | narrowed, not on the strength of this control |
| **D1.10** · **D3.7** | `canary` | **NOT A CONTROL — it is what is OWED.** Both rows name the canary as the thing that does not exist yet. No acceptance rests on it | out of set |
| **D2.13** | `corroboration` | **NOT A COMPENSATING CONTROL** — it is the gate's own mechanism (a git blob hash) being contrasted with testmon's, inside a discharged row | out of set |
| **D2.18** | `covered by` | **VOCABULARY FALSE POSITIVE** — the phrase is *"folding them together would make one look covered by the other's discharge"*, which is an argument for keeping D2.17 and D2.18 apart | out of set |

**WHY D2.21 IS NOT REOPENED, STATED SO IT CAN BE ARGUED WITH RATHER THAN INFERRED.** The standing
instruction is that a row whose control is vacuous returns to open at its original severity. The
predicate is *whose ACCEPTANCE rests on the control*, and D2.21's does not. Arm 4 was the
compensating control for the residual **while the row was OPEN**, between ARC 021 and ARC 022. The
ARC 022 DISCHARGE rests on something else entirely: `_guard_keys` replaced by `_absent_proofs`, with
the strict-subset argument (`body` went from `In ∪ NotIn ∪ Is ∪ IsNot` to `NotIn ∪ Is`) that makes
weakening structurally impossible, an eight-spelling before/after table, and the real adapter re-run
as a control with byte-identical output. That argument does not mention arm 4 and does not need it —
and the row itself already says, in bold, that the control turned out not to exist. **Reopening it
would make the ledger assert that the polarity residual is unrepaired, which the measurement says is
false**, and a manufactured residual is the same kind of untrue as a hidden one. What the sweep adds
instead is the finding above: the control was not merely absent, it was aimed at a different
property, and **that** is the reusable lesson — *a compensating control must be checked for aim
before it is checked for existence.* If the architect wants the row reopened on the rule as written
rather than on its predicate, the reopening is one edit and this paragraph is the disagreement
recorded rather than smoothed.

**A DEFECT THIS SWEEP INTRODUCED INTO THE LEDGER AND THE LEDGER'S OWN HARNESS CAUGHT WITHIN MINUTES.** The table above is the first table in this file whose left column is DEBT ROW IDS. Three of its cells were first written as bare `| D2.13 |`, `| D2.18 |` and `| D1.10 · D3.7 |`, and `check_derived_claims`'s open-row rule — `^\|\s*D[123]\.\d+\s*\|` — matched two of them, so a **commentary table about debt rows was counted as debt rows**, carrying the derived count to 64 against a hand-expected 62 and listing `D2.13, D2.18` twice. Nobody noticed by reading; the harness said so on the first run, naming both sides. Every id in the left column is now bolded, which is what keeps it out of the regex, and **this paragraph is the anchor** — a future arc adding a row-id table here must do the same. It is `debug.md` §8 failure mode #14 aimed at the ledger by an edit to the ledger, and it is the fifth time this file has been its own instrument's defect.

**WHAT THE SWEEP CHANGES BEYOND THESE ROWS.** Two rows carried a control that does not measure
(D2.20's first, D2.21's), one carried a control overstated by one instrument (D2.16), three carried
controls that hold. **The general defect is that a compensating control is named in PROSE and
nothing re-executes it** — which is the same shape as D1.14's banked demonstration going untrue in
silence. That is what the demonstration arm added to `check_derived_claims` in ARC 023 (B3) exists
to close, and D2.25 records how much of the class it does not yet reach.

## RULE OF RECORD — BINDING. The `owning module` column is AUTHORED, never inferred from prose.

**Ratified ARC 026 (B3), and it is the structural close of a contamination this ledger has now
suffered four times.**

`check_derived_claims`'s `broker_order_open_debt_rows` and `broker_datafeed_open_debt_rows` selected
rows by scanning **the whole row text** for module basenames and roster identifiers. Every repair
narrowed the vocabulary and none of them changed the mechanism, so a fifth contamination was always
one wording away:

1. **ARC 021** — `ibkr_mapping.py` hosts BOTH §2A adapters, so a row about its datafeed half counted
   as broker-order depth. Moved the count 11 → 13 while nothing touched broker-order.
2. **ARC 021** — `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py` landed on the order path (that is where §2A code lives),
   so the *datafeed adapter's own basename* became order-path vocabulary and pulled in D1.13, D3.9
   and D3.10 — three rows with no order roster identifier anywhere in them.
3. **ARC 025, and it is the sharpest example this project has produced** — D1.41 entered the
   broker-order metric because `socket.connect` contains the word `connect`. **The spy that took ARC
   024's measurement contaminated the metric measuring it.**
4. **The residual ARC 025 named and could not close** — rows are selected on PROSE ANYWHERE IN THE
   ROW, so rewording a row silently moves the count, and a row about a shared verb (`connect`,
   `disconnect`) cannot be attributed at all.

**The rule.** Every `D1.`/`D2.`/`D3.` row carries exactly ONE `owning module` token, in the last
column, written by the row's author. The metric selects on that column and on nothing else. Prose is
no longer load-bearing for attribution — reword a row freely; the count does not move.

**How a module is chosen: the artefact that must CHANGE for the row to be discharged.** Not what the
row mentions, not where the defect was observed. A row about a GATE's weakness is owned by the gate
even when the gate measures broker-datafeed, because strengthening the gate is what pays it. This is
a judgment, it is made once per row by a person, and it is visible in a diff — which is the entire
difference between this column and a regex.

**One token per row, and the constraint is deliberate.** A row spanning two modules is a row that
should be split, and permitting a list would rebuild the ambiguity the column exists to remove.

### The controlled vocabulary

An unknown token, an empty column, or a row with no column at all is a **loud ProbeError**, never a
silent exclusion — the failure mode this table replaces was precisely a row leaving a count without
anybody noticing.

| token | what it owns |
|---|---|
| `broker-order` | the §2A broker-order library: `broker_order_ibkr.py`, `broker_order_config.py`, `risks/broker_order.config.json`, the order half of `ibkr_mapping.py` |
| `broker-datafeed` | the §2A broker-datafeed library: `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py`, the datafeed half of `ibkr_mapping.py`, `Bar` and its vendor boundary |
| `broker-seam` | `scripts/broker/broker_seam.py` and `seam_simulate.py` — the port declarations, capability blocks and the simulation driver |
| `verify` | the verify family: `scripts/verify.py`, `scripts/nixverify/`, every `checks/check_*.py` and its data files, the registry, the drivers, `status` |
| `hooks` | `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, the installed hook suite, the pinned hook environments, `scripts/runtime_gate.py` |
| `node` | the box: systemd units and slices, OS packages, hardware identity, worktree provisioning |
| `ibgateway` | Xvfb and IB Gateway as services, their API surface, entitlements and session state |
| `database` | the PostgreSQL cluster, its roles, and the schemas in `nix_db_schema_spec.docx` |
| `credentials` | credential sealing and `state/encrypt_credentials.py` |
| `docs` | rows whose subject is a document — citations, numbering, specification text |
| `capture` | the §2A capture path: `scripts/capture.py`, `scripts/nixbus/` — the core map, the ipc:// state bus and the SPSC price ring — and `scripts/feed_kill_drill.py`, the §13 V24 kill drill that exercises all three (ARC 027 C; the token's coverage was extended rather than an eleventh token minted, because the drill has no subject of its own — it is an instrument pointed at this path) |
| `limiter` | the Risk Engine / Limiter: `scripts/nixrisk/` — the frozen seam, the two-phase gate pass, the reservation ledger and the financial-picture publisher (ARC 028; minted because `scripts/nixrisk/` is a new module with no token, and an unattributed open row is a loud `ProbeError` rather than a silent exclusion) |

### Stated per-module tally — THE SECOND SOURCE, and why it exists

`check_derived_claims` requires every claim to have **at least two sources**; a lone number is
unchecked by construction. Before this arc the two sources for a module's depth were the frozen
spec's roster and the seam's roster — two independent restatements of one vocabulary. **Selecting on
an authored column removes that independence: two sources reading the same column would agree
always, and the claim would be vacuous.** That would have been a defect introduced by the repair.

So the pair is restated in the shape `check_debt_open_items` already uses: the ROW SCAN is derived,
the table below is STATED, and a machine compares them (doctrine B.7). Add a row and forget the
tally and the claim goes RED naming both numbers. **Nothing is lost:** the spec-roster-versus-seam-
roster property still has an owner — claim `spec_2a_broker_order_elements` — and doctrine C.9 says
one property, one instrument.

**Counts are OPEN rows only, by the bold-span rule below.** ARC 028 (D) brought `docs` and
`verify` back into agreement with the row scan and recorded the mechanism's real reach as **D3.78**:
only `broker-order` and `broker-datafeed` are compared by a claim, and a vocabulary token the tally
OMITS entirely (`capture`) is not rejected at all.

| module | open rows |
|---|---|
| broker-order | 9 |
| broker-datafeed | 7 |
| broker-seam | 3 |
| verify | 63 |
| hooks | 3 |
| node | 5 |
| ibgateway | 3 |
| database | 2 |
| credentials | 2 |
| docs | 6 |

## D1 — Environment changes made, no check written

Each is an environment change that has already happened on this node.

| # | subject | changed in | owner | owning module |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1.1 | sealed credentials (`systemd-creds` TPM2) | ARC 008 design | unassigned — blocked on the Fernet→systemd-creds migration arc | credentials |
| D1.2 | `nix-trading.slice` core pinning (`AllowedCPUs=0-5`) | ARC 006 | unassigned | node |
| D1.3 | systemd units exist, enabled, and `ExecStart` matches the spec'd interpreter | ARC 006/008 | unassigned — `scripts/tests/test_systemd_units.py` reads the unit *files*; nothing reads the running system | node |
| D1.4 | `trade_history` schema conformance vs `nix_db_schema_spec.docx` | ARC 006 | unassigned | database |
| D1.5 | Postgres cluster present, running, roles separated | ARC 006 | unassigned | database |
| D1.6 | `unattended-upgrades` enabled and healthy | ARC 006 | unassigned — §7 makes this the *only* owner of OS patching, so its health is load-bearing | node |
| D1.7 | `git` present (floor component, verify-only per §3) | ARC 006 | unassigned | node |
| D1.10 | pre-commit hook suite installed and each hook actually capable of failing | ARC 006 / ARC 010 | **still open, narrowed again by ARC 018.** Per-hook can-fail now stands at **8 of 8 demonstrated, 0 partial**: `ruff-check`/`pylint`/`mypy`/`complexipy` D3.2 (ARC 015), `bandit (production)` D3.1 (ARC 010), `bandit (tests)` D3.6 (ARC 017), `pytest-affected` D3.4 (ARC 017, re-run ARC 018 after the gate was rebuilt), `ruff-format` D3.5 (ARC 018, adopted `--check`). What remains owed for D1.10 itself is different from any D3 row: nothing *asserts* the suite is installed or that its hook set is intact. `pre-commit run` is invoked by a human or by the git hook; there is no gate that fails when `.pre-commit-config.yaml` loses a hook, when `core.hooksPath` is unset, or when the pinned `rev:` moves to an environment that scans nothing. **ARC 018 re-measured that last condition rather than repeating the claim, and it is NOT acceptable standing risk — it is owed, and this row owns it.** The pre-ARC-010 bandit environment (repo rev `2d0b675`) is still in `~/.cache/pre-commit` and still reproduces the ARC 006 vacuum verbatim: run against this tree's production Python it reported `exception while scanning file` for **every** file, zero findings, **exit 0**; handed a file containing `subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True)` it skipped that too and still exited 0, while the pinned `1.9.4` environment flags it High and exits 1. A `rev:` pin is a declaration; the declaration is currently the only thing standing between this repo and a silent green, and no instrument checks it. Non-vacuity per hook was captured by hand (ARC 017, re-derived ARC 018) — capturing it by hand is not a gate. **NARROWED ARC 019, NOT DISCHARGED.** `checks/check_hook_suite.py` now asserts the *effective* state this row said nothing asserted, in four arms, all derived: (1) the hook file at the path **git itself** resolves (`git rev-parse --git-path hooks`, which is the only spelling correct in both a plain repo and a linked worktree — measured in a worktree whose `.git` is a *file*, where the private git dir holds no `hooks/` and the shared common dir does) exists, is executable, and is pre-commit's own script per `pre_commit.commands.install_uninstall.is_our_script`; (2) the installed hook's embedded `ARGS=(hook-impl --config=… --hook-type=…)` names *this* config, so a real, executable, genuinely-pre-commit hook pointed at a different file is caught; (3) every hook the config resolves to — via pre-commit's own `all_hooks`/`Store`, never a snapshot list, so a hook added later is covered automatically — has an installed environment and selects a **non-empty** file set under pre-commit's own `Classifier`, unless `always_run`; (4) each pinned repo's environment is the store row keyed to the rev the config pins, and every *other* resident environment for the same repo is named as an advisory on every run. Can-fail proven on all of (1)–(3) with a CONTROL either side, driven against a **throwaway repository** rather than `~/nix/.git/hooks/`, which is shared by every linked worktree and would have disarmed two sibling agents mid-arc. **What keeps this row open is the `rev:` question this row itself raised.** Arm (4) proves the *selected* environment is the pinned one — the ARC 006 bandit env at `1.8.6` is still resident and is demonstrably not what the hooks resolve to — but nothing here proves the *pinned* environment is non-vacuous, because nothing structural distinguishes a bandit that scans 21 files from one that raises on all 21 and exits 0. That needs a per-hook canary, which is the D3 series' shape and is opened as **D3.7**. Also still open and named beside the gate: `git commit --no-verify` (no static gate can see a bypass — doctrine's `bank.sh` territory, D2.4), and hook types other than `pre-commit`, which this repo does not configure today | hooks |

Revisit when broker-order code exists and can host the probe | ibgateway |
| D1.12 | **Reboot behaviour of `nix-xvfb.service` / `nix-ibgateway.service`** | ARC 011 | **still open, narrowed by ARC 012.** The cutover is done — both processes are systemd-owned (cgroup `/system.slice/nix-*.service`, API socket served by the unit's own MainPID) and `verify.py` is green. What remains unproven is only that systemd starts them **at boot**: `systemctl is-enabled` is a declaration, not evidence. A reboot was offered as a separate authorization in ARC 012 and declined (second IB Key tap). Discharge: reboot, then run `check_ibgateway_service` **before anyone touches the console** — a human logging in first creates the very state the check must observe independently. **CAPTURE MECHANISM BUILT ARC 019, NOT YET ARMED, ROW STAYS OPEN.** `scripts/d1_12_reboot_capture.py` plus `scripts/nix-reboot-capture.service` run the check from a systemd oneshot at boot so the verdict is taken before a human can reach the console. The load-bearing half is not the verdict: it records **evidence that nobody was there** — `who`, `loginctl list-sessions`, and uptime-at-capture against a 300 s ceiling — and writes `"trustworthy": false` with reasons when that precondition fails, because a verdict taken by hand ten minutes in cannot distinguish "it came back on its own" from "it came back because I was here", and it fails in the direction that looks like success. Demonstrated able to say no without a plant: run interactively during ARC 019 it returned `NOT TRUSTWORTHY`, naming three active `loginctl` sessions and a 744803.6 s uptime. `is-enabled` is recorded under a key that says DECLARATION_ONLY so it can never be mistaken for the evidence. **What remains owed is the arming and the reboot itself** — installing the unit needs root and the reboot is the operator's call; absence of a capture file after a boot is itself the finding, since it means the unit did not run. **MECHANISM DEFECT FOUND AND REPAIRED ARC 020, BEFORE ARMING, AND IT WOULD HAVE WASTED THE TAP.** Every reference to the Gateway unit in both `scripts/d1_12_reboot_capture.py` and `scripts/nix-reboot-capture.service` read `ibgateway.service`, which **is not a unit on this system** — the units are `nix-ibgateway.service` and `nix-xvfb.service`. The failure is silent in the direction that looks like a real finding: `systemctl show` on an unknown unit does **not** error, it returns `ActiveState=inactive SubState=dead Result=success` at rc=0, measured this arc and **byte-identical to what the real unit returns while genuinely stopped** — the only distinguishing field is `LoadState`, which the capture did not request, and an empty `ExecMainStartTimestamp`. Armed as written, the reboot would have recorded "the Gateway did not come back" about a unit that does not exist, and would have spent the operator tap to do it. `After=` was equally stale, and systemd treats an `After=` on an unknown unit as a silent no-op, so the ordering constraint the unit's own comment explains was **absent**. **Why it survived ARC 019:** that arc demonstrated this file could say no by running it interactively, and it correctly returned `NOT TRUSTWORTHY` naming three `loginctl` sessions and a 744803.6 s uptime — but that exercises the **operator-presence** half only. The unit half was never driven, so the demonstration proved the part that worked and the defect rode through underneath it. That is `debug.md` §5.7 instrument-audit debt about the instrument built to discharge this very row. **Repair:** both unit names corrected, `nix-xvfb.service` added (this row's subject names **both** units and the capture only ever looked at one), `LoadState` and `Id` now recorded **first** so a future rename reports `not-found` loudly instead of measuring nothing, and the API-reachability check renamed `check_ibgateway_service_NOT_THE_D1_12_VERDICT` because IB Gateway does not serve its API until an IB Key login completes — a boot capture will show it unreachable no matter how correctly systemd started the process, and the D1.12 evidence is `ActiveState`, not that. Driven interactively after the repair: both units resolve `LoadState=loaded`, and the capture still refuses trust on operator presence. **ARMED ARC 020, AND THE ARMING WAS VALIDATED UNDER SYSTEMD RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `/etc/systemd/system/nix-reboot-capture.service` installed and `enabled` (`UnitFileState=enabled`, `LoadState=loaded`, symlinked into `multi-user.target.wants`). Then **started once by hand to prove it executes**, because an armed unit that fails at boot for an environment reason wastes the reboot exactly as thoroughly as a wrong unit name: `Result=success`, `ExecMainStatus=0`, it resolved the venv interpreter, wrote its capture to `logs/d1_12_reboot_capture/`, and **still refused trust**, naming three `loginctl` sessions and a 771853.7 s uptime against the 300 s ceiling. That is the whole mechanism driven end to end under systemd as `bbt`, with only the untouched-console precondition unmet — and it is unmet for the correct reason. The validation capture was then **deleted**, so the directory is empty: after the next boot, an empty directory is itself the finding, since it means the unit did not run. **What remains owed is only the reboot, which is the operator's call, and the IB Key tap after it.** The operator elected to arm now and reboot later; the capture fires on whatever boot comes next, with no further action needed. **Row stays open — no reboot has been taken** | ibgateway |

The gate drives every callable annotated `-> MarketDataMode` with the sentinel (0 -> UNKNOWN and NOT REALTIME), the vendor default (1 -> REALTIME and NOT UNKNOWN) and the downgrade (3 -> DELAYED and NOT DELAYED_FROZEN), and then asserts the leg the other three do not imply: **legs 1 and 2 must produce DIFFERENT values**. It also proves structurally that the sentinel is WRITTEN (an observer that can express the distinction over a field nobody sentinels never sees leg 1 in production) and that no name handed to `reqMarketDataType` supplies the granted value. **The vendor default is MEASURED on every run, never restated**: the pinned distributions in `checks/pinned_deps.json` are searched for the class carrying the field and its dataclass default is read off the wheel — `ib_async.Ticker.marketDataType = 1` at ARC 021. Control restored byte-identical by sha256 and PASS returned. **HALF ONE, STILL OWED: the gate has never bound to a real adapter.** It binds when `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py` lands and is re-proven there — see **D3.9**. **HALF TWO, STILL OWED: the LIVE re-confirmation — and it is a re-confirmation, not a first observation.** ARC 013 already performed this exact sentinel experiment live on this account and banked it (`sessions/SESSION.md`, ARC 013): mode 1 granted nothing at all with error 354, mode 3 granted 3, mode 4 **silently granted as 3**, and the sentinel was what distinguished the unset field from a grant. So the live half is **D1.33**, and it is worded as *re-confirm on a current session* rather than *never observed*, because those are materially different debts | broker-datafeed |

Split from D1.13 because it discharges in a different arc, and it stays owed even after a real-time feed arrives at Tradovate. **GATE BUILT ARC 021 (B2), NOT DISCHARGED.** `checks/check_datafeed_bar_seal.py` is registered and reports **CANNOT_MEASURE (exit 2)** on this tree, for a reason worth stating precisely: two of its four arms CAN measure today — `FeedLag` is derived as a published type off `feed_lag()`'s return annotation, is constructed twice from synthesised values differing in one field, and is proven to refuse a field write while keeping value equality — **and they are deliberately not allowed to carry a PASS**, because *the datafeed's one published value type is frozen* is not this row's property, and letting it report green would close a debt that is still owed. Two arcs of `13/13` passed over this sentence going untrue; a third cannot. | D1.18 | **The order-rejection ack carries an IBKR numeric error code across the seam** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 017 | unassigned. `_on_ib_error` calls `self._ack_once(cid, REJECTED, reason=f"{errorCode}: {errorString}")`, so a vendor integer reaches the seam inside `on_ack(reason)`. Invariant 2 says no IBKR code crosses the seam; ARC 017 enforced that on the **session** event (1100/1101/1102 removed from every session `reason` and moved to adapter-internal logging) but deliberately did **not** extend it to acks, because the parent scoped the change to the session path and because `on_ack(reason)` is the declared human-readable provenance channel where error 201's margin figure has real diagnostic value. So this is a genuine tension between invariant 2 and the provenance channel, not an oversight. Discharge alongside the Limiter, which is the first component that will actually consume an ack reason and can settle whether a structured rejection cause is owed the way `UP_DATA_LOSS` was owed for sessions. **Reported rather than substituting a decision.** **DISCHARGED ARC 018.** The deferral rationale in this row was **factually wrong and that error was the whole basis for deferring**: the Limiter is not "the first component that will actually consume an ack reason" — three consumers already read the ack `reason` slot by substring match (`test_broker_order.py:626`, `:1773`, `:1813`), and two of them match it to derive a *fact* (whether an ack was synthesised by §2c). So the field is neither structurally protected from programmatic consumption nor merely unconsumed; it is consumed today. Repair follows the `UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent: `RejectCategory` {`UNKNOWN`, `INSUFFICIENT_MARGIN`, `NOT_TRADABLE`, `VENUE_UNAVAILABLE`} is carried as a **keyword-only** `reject_category` on `on_ack`, so §2A's positional shape is untouched and the addition is visibly an addition; the frozen spec is not edited. `reason` keeps the full human text **including** the IBKR code, so error 201's margin figure — this row's own reason for the tension — is preserved. Invariant 2 is asserted mechanically two independent ways per rejection (the driving code appears in no structured field; and no structured field contains any digit at all), across an AST-derived list of every REJECTED-ack emission site rather than a hand-written one. Categories are justified by *distinct Limiter behaviour*, not by re-spelling IBKR's error list, and anything unmapped falls to `UNKNOWN` — including a 201 whose text does not carry the measured money wording, because IBKR's 201 is a wrapper and keying on the bare integer would make every 201 read as a money problem | discharged ARC 018 | broker-order |
| D1.19 | **Ack provenance — venue-vs-synthesised — is carried only as free text** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 018 | unassigned. Split out of D1.18 rather than folded into it, because it is a *different* defect that happens to share a field. `broker_order_ibkr.py` deliberately emits a genuine venue acceptance with **no** reason string, and marks a §2c-synthesised ack with the word `synthesised` in `reason`; the adapter's own comment endorses this as the mechanism. Three test sites read it back by substring (`test_broker_order.py:626`, `:1773`, `:1813`). That is `debug.md` §7.4's stale literal anchor — a *fact* a consumer needs, recoverable only by matching English in a free-text field. It is **not** an invariant-2 violation: `synthesised` is a Nix word, not a vendor spelling, which is exactly why ARC 018 fixed the vendor half (D1.18) and named this half instead of quietly widening its own scope. Discharge with the same shape that discharged D1.18 — a structured `AckProvenance {VENUE, SYNTHESISED}` beside `reject_category` — at the point a consumer needs it | unassigned — discharges with the Limiter or with any arc touching the §2c synthesis path | broker-order |

Found by the C1 gate on its first run and reported rather than reddened. All six cite the check contract by filename and then name a section it does not have — sections 9.1 and 9.2 at `scripts/verify.py:6` and `:7`, section 9.1 at `scripts/nixverify/__init__.py:1`, sections 9.3 and 9.4 at `scripts/tests/test_loader.py:1`, section 9.5 at `scripts/tests/test_systemd_units.py:1`, and section 9.1 at `install.sh:126`. **That document has no 9.1 through 9.5**: its section 9 is "Engine invariants" and its subsections are unnumbered. The numbers are carried over from the ARC 008 self-authored reconstruction that ARC 010 renamed *into* `nix_check_contract.md`, so the rename moved the filename and left the section numbering behind — the identical shape to the 2.1 phantom, one document over. | D1.20 | **`_mirror_stale` latches across a successful reconnect** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 018 | unassigned. The flag is observable and correct at all three write sites and is read by three assertions, but `connect()` (`broker_order_ibkr.py`) discards `_rebuild_mirror()`'s verdict and never clears it, so a re-read that failed once stays `True` across a full reconnect that *did* rebuild successfully. It fails toward "suspect", which is the safe direction, so this is not urgent — but a consumer gating new entries on it would never resume trading after one failed re-read, making it a one-way door. It has no consumer today only because the Limiter does not exist. **Whoever builds that consumer must fix the latch in the same motion**; the required consumer semantics were written down in ARC 018 while the reasoning was fresh (treat `True` as HALT-class for new entries; do **not** let it suppress a protective flatten; pair it with `UP_DATA_LOSS` as level-to-edge). **ADAPTER HALF CLOSED ARC 019 (sub-agent A) — NARROWED, NOT DISCHARGED.** `connect()` now derives both `_mirror_stale` and the published session state from `_rebuild_mirror()`'s verdict, so a successful rebuild clears the flag and a failed one sets it; non-vacuity is proven by asserting the flag genuinely `True` immediately before the clearing reconnect, so "cleared" is a `True → False` transition and not a vacuous `False → False`. The same missing assignment was hiding a louder defect, fixed in the same motion: a **failed** cold-start rebuild used to publish plain `SessionState.UP` — the adapter reporting a healthy session over a mirror it did not rebuild, on the path §4 designates as ground truth. It now publishes `UP_DATA_LOSS`. A second laundering path was closed with it: a 1102 ("restored, data intact") arriving while `_mirror_stale` was already `True` used to wash a suspect mirror clean, and the level now wins over the edge. All emission sites are held to one mechanically-asserted invariant: plain `SessionState.UP` is published ONLY while `_mirror_stale` is `False`. **THE CONSUMER HALF REMAINS OPEN and is why this row does not close** | unassigned — consumer half discharges with the Limiter (R2) | broker-order |
| D1.22 | **A send verb cannot tell the caller whether the order reached the venue** | ARC 019 A1, measured | unassigned — discharges with the Limiter (R2). **NARROWED ARC 020 (A7), NOT DISCHARGED.** The adapter previously gave a consumer no way to know it was absorbing, so absorption had to be inferred from silence; `send_backlog()` now reports `vendor_queue_depth` (`ib_async.Client._msgQ`) and `transport_write_buffer_bytes` (the asyncio transport) as observable adapter state, with a `measured: bool` CANNOT-MEASURE floor so an unreadable pipe returns `None` and never `0` — a healthy idle pipe and a blind one stay distinguishable (`debug.md` failure mode #10). Every read is defensive and the method never raises, asserted against a `__len__` that does. **Deliberately NOT added to `ORDER_PORT_VERBS`**: a port verb binds every vendor, and whether a second venue can report its own queue depth is unmeasured — declaring a cross-vendor obligation from a sample of one is how a seam acquires a requirement nobody checked. Promotion is a `BrokerCapabilities` question for vendor two. **What stays open is the whole of the original row**: no bounded-queue policy was implemented (where the bound sits and what happens at it is the Limiter's), the resolution is still §4's pending-timeout state machine plus `query_order_status`, and **the repair is still never a resend**. Observability is not resolution. The send path provably does not block (16/16 verb×socket-condition cells returned under 0.0033 s against a real loopback socket with the real vendor serialiser), but the *reason* is that it **absorbs**: asyncio's transport buffers what the kernel will not take and `ib_async.Client.sendMsg` holds its own `_msgQ` in front of that. Measured directly — 200 `place_order` calls into a verified-saturated pipe returned in 0.032 s total, leaving 155 messages queued, 10204 B buffered and **zero bytes delivered to the peer**. Every one of those calls returned normally and none is known to have reached the venue. This is **not** a defect in the adapter and the repair is **never** a resend (§2A:71, §4:241, §12A:830). §4 already specifies the resolution — the pending-timeout state machine plus `query_order_status` — and both belong to the Limiter. The obligation is therefore a **consumer** one: treat a returned `place_order` as *submitted*, never as *accepted by the venue*; run a pending-ack timeout against it (`PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`, §12A:830); resolve expiry by **querying** status. A bounded-queue-depth decision belongs at that point too, since today's queues are unbounded and a long stall trades a block for unbounded memory and an unbounded backlog of stale orders | broker-order |
| D1.23 | **A cancelled `connect()` leaves an adapter that accepts orders and can never report on them** | ARC 017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-01) | **discharged ARC 020 (A2).** `connect()` now wraps session establishment in `except BaseException` and **re-raises**: cancellation is caught deliberately, because `CancelledError` is a `BaseException` and every `except Exception` in the file missed it, and it is re-raised untouched, because swallowing it would let `asyncio.wait_for` return normally from a call that did not complete — a different and worse defect than the one being fixed. A cancelled or failed `connect()` now leaves the adapter **refusing orders** (`_connected = False`, so `_require_session` raises `BrokerNotConnected`), with `_mirror_stale = True` because the rebuild verdict was never computed, and per-order state cleared. The refusal is observable on two channels, both asserted: `place_order` raises and nothing reaches the venue, and a cancelled *reconnect* over a previously-UP session publishes a genuine `DOWN` edge. A cancelled *first* connect publishes nothing, which is now correct rather than a gap — §2A defines `on_session` as connectivity **transitions**, and the caller's notification is the `CancelledError` it is about to receive. Non-vacuity asserts the cancel landed inside the intended window (`_connected is True` so not before `connectAsync`, `_startup_complete is False` so not after publish, and the rebuild's venue call proven entered). Can-fail four outputs; the `strict=True` xfail was removed in the same motion as the fix. Original finding, retained: `connect()` sets `_connected = True`, *then* awaits `_rebuild_mirror()`. `CancelledError` is a `BaseException`, so `_rebuild_mirror`'s `except Exception` does not catch it and nothing unwinds `_connected`. Any caller bounding the call with `asyncio.wait_for` — the ordinary way to bound a verb that talks to a venue — lands in that window. Blast radius measured: `place_order` **succeeds and reaches the venue** while `sink.acks == []`, `sink.fills == []`, `_mirror == {}`, and no `on_session` was ever published, so the consumer has been told nothing. The order path is live and mute. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail in `scripts/tests/test_broker_tier3.py`, so a repair turns the suite RED until the marker is removed in the same motion. **Not** addressed by D1.20's adapter fix — the rebuild verdict is never computed when the await is cancelled. Needs a decision about what a partially-completed `connect()` leaves behind, not a one-line fix | broker-order |
| D1.24 | **Per-order state is never released, and is not cleared across a session boundary** | ARC 014/017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B; T3-01b, T3-02, T3-07, T3-08, T3-13) | **discharged ARC 020 (A1)**, and it was landed FIRST because D1.27(b)'s ownership gate is only sound on top of it. **Clearing happens at BOTH ends, and neither is redundant.** Teardown (`disconnect`, `_on_ib_disconnected`, `_abandon_session`) is where state is *released*, because teardown is the boundary a 03:00 Gateway restart actually arrives on and nothing guarantees a prompt reconnect — waiting for `connect()` would leave a live vendor `Order` object addressable for an unbounded interval. `connect()` clears again *before* `connectAsync`, because a process that connects without ever having disconnected has no teardown to have cleared it, and because that clear is what makes the ownership gate sound. **Retention rule:** terminal orders (`filled`/`cancelled`/`rejected`) are released after `terminal_order_retention_ms` = 20000 (10 × `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`), boot-validated to exceed both `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` and `FILL_TIMEOUT_MS`. It cannot be zero for two independent §4 reasons: §4 "Failure resolution" resolves a pending timeout by *querying*, so the order must outlive the interval before the Limiter asks; and §4 "Partial fill" anticipates an execution arriving *after* a terminal transition. Pruning is lazy and amortised O(1) — no timer, no task. `_seen_execs` is released *with* the id map rather than separately: once the vendor mapping is gone a replayed execution is refused one step earlier, so the dedupe is subsumed, not lost. **THE IN-FLIGHT-AT-DROP CASE, which is the one that owed a real answer:** an order non-terminal when the session ended is neither deleted nor kept. Its per-order state is released and a `_Tombstone` retained holding `{client_order_id, last_known_state, cumulative_qty, session_seq}`. `query_order_status` returns `state="indeterminate"`, `terminal=False`, and the observed `cumulative_qty` floor — **§4:241's own third outcome, which this adapter previously could not reach at all.** It is not a lie in either direction: not `working` (a claim about a venue we can no longer see), not `unknown` (which would invite the Limiter to re-mint the id of an order that may be live). The id is neither re-mintable nor cancellable while the answer is outstanding; both raise `BrokerSeamError` naming the ended session and pointing at `query_order_status`. Terminal ids *are* released, so the refusal covers only ids that genuinely cannot be reused. `OrderStatus.state` gains `indeterminate` as a **declared Nix addition** on the `feed_lag()`/`UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent. Non-vacuity asserts the maps were genuinely populated before the boundary (non-empty → empty, not empty → empty) and that the fake genuinely recycled the vendor id, so the refusal costs something. Two `strict=True` xfails removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained — one shared repair, five observed symptoms. Measured: after **200 fully-closed lifecycles with the mirror netting flat**, `_neutral 200 · _orders 200 · _trades 200 · _to_ib 200 · _from_ib 200 · _acked 200 · _seen_execs 200` — nothing is pruned on a terminal transition (§12 non-stop operation; §13 objective 22 is a multi-day soak asserting memory stability). `connect()` clears `_to_ib`/`_from_ib`/`_acked`/`_cancelled` because IBKR ids reset across the Gateway's restart, but **not** `_orders`/`_trades`/`_neutral`/`_seen_execs`, and the consequences are concrete: (a) **`cancel_order` on a pre-restart id puts a live foreign order's `orderId` on the wire** — no race needed, just place / restart / place / cancel-the-first; (b) `query_order_status` returns the dead session's cached `working` forever, and §4:241 names exactly three outcomes (confirmed / cancelled / indeterminate) of which this adapter can reach two, because the only branch that can express uncertainty is reachable only when the trade is **absent**; (c) a Limiter minting deterministic neutral ids is blocked after a restart on an id that means nothing at the venue; (d) a futile cancel of a terminal order reaches the wire silently. Discharge needs a **retention policy** — how long must a terminal order stay queryable through `query_order_status`? — which is a §4 question, not a free deletion. Two of these are held open by `strict=True` xfails | broker-order |
| D1.25 | **Session state is published from a deferred task that never re-checks the session** | ARC 017 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-02b) | **discharged ARC 020 (A3)** by **two** independent mechanisms, each with its own can-fail, because one proved insufficient. (1) A single emission choke point, `_publish_session`, through which every `on_session` path now routes, enforcing two fail-closed rules: no UP-class state while `_connected` is False, and no non-transition `DOWN`. (2) A session epoch: `_revalidate_then_publish` carries the session it was scheduled in, and a rebuild completing into a *different* session publishes nothing and does not write `_mirror_stale` — a dead session's verdict must not land on a live session's flag. The verdict now gates **whether** to publish, not merely the `reason` string. The invariant is asserted **once, across all emission sites**: the test derives the emission-site list from the AST, asserts it has exactly one member named `_publish_session`, then wraps the sink and drives all seven publishing paths, asserting no UP-class state was ever published while `_connected` was False. AST scope is derived from `inspect.getsourcefile(IBKRBrokerOrder)` — the subject names its own file — to avoid failure mode #14. **A finding about this arc's own instrument, recorded because §7.12 asks it of every gate at the point it is built:** the first can-fail plant (removing the epoch check) **did not perturb** — the suite stayed green, because the only test driving that path drove the *disconnected* case, which the choke point catches on its own. Two mechanisms, one observable, and the weaker sufficed for everything being driven; that is failure mode #1 about a gate built in this arc. A traversal was added for the one sequence the choke point structurally cannot see — reconcile scheduled in session N, N torn down, N+1 established and UP — and the plant then perturbed. `strict=True` xfail removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained: `_on_data_loss_restore` schedules `_revalidate_then_publish` and returns; a `disconnect()` landing in that gap is enough to publish `UP_DATA_LOSS` (`is_up=True`) over a torn-down session, observed with `_connected=False`. The rebuild verdict selects only the `reason` **string** — it never gates the publish at all. `SessionState.is_up` is documented as the canonical spelling of "is the session usable", so this reads True over a session that is not, and it fails toward **resuming**, the direction §14 forbids. Adjacent to but **not** fixed by D1.20's ARC 019 work, which closed the cold-start and 1102 laundering paths and did not touch the deferred-task path. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail | broker-order |
| D1.26 | **Two overlapping `query_positions()` calls lose the newer snapshot** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-10) | **discharged ARC 020 (A5).** Guard chosen: **a monotonic sequence taken per read *before* the await; a read resolving with a number lower than the newest already-resolved read is discarded.** Serialising reads behind a lock was considered and **rejected**, and the reason is the protective path: a lock makes one read *wait* for another, and `_rebuild_mirror`'s completion is what clears `_mirror_stale`, which is what `flatten` sizes against — so a lock would put an unrelated caller's round trip in front of the protective path's input, which §2A's "`flatten` must not block" forbids. **No await was added to the protective path, and none could have been**: the guard is one `next()` on an `itertools.count` and one integer comparison, both on the already-async read path, and `flatten()` does not call `query_positions` at all — it reads `_mirror` from memory, as it always has. The sequence advances on *resolution*, including when the pre-existing fills-guard declines to apply, because a skip is itself a newer decision about the field. The **returned list is deliberately unguarded**: a caller gets the snapshot it asked for, and the guard protects the shared field two readers can corrupt for each other. Non-vacuity asserts from the fake's own record that both reads genuinely entered, that neither had completed at setup, and that the completion order really was reversed — the trap ARC 019's traversal hit twice, where a sequence never actually interleaved. `strict=True` xfail removed in the same motion. Original finding, retained: `self._mirror` is assigned wholesale with no ordering guard, so a read issued first but completing last overwrites a newer venue-confirmed state; observed `mirror={}` after the venue had already confirmed `+3`. The module produces the concurrent second read **itself** — `_on_data_loss_restore` schedules one and §4 makes a cold-start one mandatory. The same method already guards the *adjacent* case, snapshotting `_seen_execs` across the await with a comment saying a fill landing between request and assignment would be silently erased: fills are guarded, a concurrent read of the same field is not. This is the protective path's only input. Held open by a `strict=True` xfail | broker-order |
| D1.27 | **Two `flatten()` sequences have no specified correct behaviour — SPEC GAPS, not code defects** | found ARC 019 (sub-agent B, T3-04 and T3-05) | **RE-DISPOSED ARC 020 — STILL OPEN, NOT DISCHARGED.** This cell read *"architect, not ARC 020"* until ARC 020 began, and that was **stale rather than wrong**: it was written in ARC 019 before the operator had ruled, and ARC 020's brief carries **both** ratified operator rulings — one for the idempotence gap (a) and one for the protective-flatten-during-reconnect gap (b) — which are exactly the two things this row said only the architect could supply. The rulings were therefore available to this arc, and this row pointed the reader away from them. **Disposition now:** sub-agent A implements both in ARC 020 as **declared Nix additions** following the `feed_lag()` / `UP_DATA_LOSS` precedent, and each ruling is recorded verbatim, attributed as an operator ruling in ARC 020 rather than as spec text, in the pending-amendment record. **The row stays open, and the reason is the row's own subject.** What it records is a gap in a **frozen document**, and behaviour landing in Nix code does not make that document say anything. Only a v1.4 closes it, that is an architect action, and until it lands the specified behaviour and the implemented behaviour are two different things — which is the condition this row exists to keep visible. Discharge is a v1.4 carrying both rulings; the owner of the discharge is the architect, and the owner of the *implementation* was this arc. ARC 019 deliberately did not invent the answers, and did not have to. **BOTH HALVES ARE NOW IMPLEMENTED, AND THIS ROW IS STILL OPEN — the disagreement was real and is recorded rather than smoothed.** ARC 020's sub-agent A reported (a) and (b) as *discharged*; sub-agent C, which owns this ledger, re-disposed the row as *still open*. **Phase 4 adjudicated in C's favour, and the reason is the row's own subject.** What this row records is a gap in a **frozen document**. Behaviour landing in Nix code does not make that document say anything, and closing the row on an implementation would leave the specified behaviour and the implemented behaviour silently divergent — which is the exact condition this row exists to keep visible. Prohibition 1 and the pending-amendment procedure both place the v1.4 with the architect. (a) landed as the bounded flatten idempotency window, `flatten_idempotency_window_ms` = 2000, **derived as = `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`** (§12A:830, "~1–2s", upper end taken) and living in `risks/broker_order.config.json` rather than as a literal on the protective path: the window suppresses a repeat protective flatten for exactly as long as the Limiter is still waiting for the first flatten's ack, and at that instant the §4 pending-timeout machinery takes the question over, so suppressing past it would let the adapter refuse to protect while nothing else was protecting either. Four cross-knob boot-validation rules, all can-fail demonstrated; a missing or invalid config raises rather than defaulting. (b) landed as the ownership discriminator in `_startup_admits`. Both rulings are recorded **verbatim** in the code and in `docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md`, attributed as operator rulings issued in ARC 020 rather than as spec text. (a) **`flatten()` is not idempotent:** two protective flattens over one `+2` mirror emit **−4** of market orders, because `place_order` does not decrement the mirror and only a fill does. §2A's `flatten(symbol \| all)` bullet defines the verb as "market-close a position (protective path; must not block)" and is **silent on repeat invocation**; §4 lists six *independent* protective triggers, unconditional, so two firing in one cycle is a designed shape rather than a caller error, and §4's "one in-flight action per strategy" governs strategy signals, not Limiter-side protective exits. The section that would have to say is §2A's `flatten` bullet or §4 "Exits (dual authority)" — **neither currently does.** (b) **A protective `flatten()` during reconnect fires into a shut startup gate:** the fan-out proceeds (`_connected` is set before the rebuild) and every ack and fill for those orders is refused by the still-closed gate. §14's "the exit/protective path has zero wire/delivery dependency" is honoured literally — nothing blocked — and not in the sense that matters, because the outcome is unobservable. The section that would have to say is §4 "Boot / known-state discipline", which gates *registration* on the cold-start query and is silent on a protective exit during session **re-establishment**, which is exactly when one is most likely | broker-order |
| D1.28 | **The protective path cannot report that it failed to achieve its purpose** | ARC 014 code, found ARC 019 (sub-agent B; T3-06, T3-11, T3-12) | **NARROWED ARC 020 (A6), NOT DISCHARGED — the adapter half is done and the consumer half is untouched.** All three surprises are now observable. `flatten` produces a `FlattenAttempt` record following `place_order`'s local-receipt pattern — the §2A `-> None` signature is untouched, the record is retained internally and read back through `last_flatten_attempt()` / `flatten_attempts()` (bounded ring, depth 64) — carrying per-attempt `intents`, `suppressed` (with prior cid, prior attempt seq, age and window), `no_position`, `failures`, and `mirror_stale` captured **at attempt time**. `is_silent_no_op` is the readable spelling of (b). An unheld symbol logs loudly and records but deliberately **does not raise**: §4's flatten-on-uncertainty "may hit nothing OR close a real position", so raising would make the safe case look like a fault, and failure mode #11 asks for an observable, not a throw. For (c), `disconnect()`'s teardown stays unconditional and best-effort while only the *emission* becomes edge-correct, enforced at `_publish_session` so no future site can reopen it; UP-class states are deliberately not collapsed, because a repeated `UP_DATA_LOSS` carries a fresh reconciliation obligation. **WHY THIS IS NOT DISCHARGED: nothing reads `FlattenAttempt`.** The record is the adapter's declaration of **intent**, never a claim about the venue — D1.22 proves intent and arrival are different facts — and intent-versus-outcome reconciliation is the Limiter's (R2). A reconciler was explicitly not built. Consumer obligations recorded: `suppressed` means *a protective action was requested and deliberately not sent*, not that it was sent; `is_silent_no_op` together with `mirror_stale` is the flatten-on-uncertainty trigger; `intents` must be reconciled against venue truth. This consumer half now sits alongside D1.20's. Original finding, retained — consumer-adjacent. Three surprises that are individually defensible and together mean a caller cannot distinguish success from silence. (a) `flatten()` sizes from a mirror snapshot and never re-checks: a fill landing one microsecond after the fan-out leaves the protective order under-sized — observed mirror `+2` against a `SELL 1` — and **nothing reports that the protective action fell short**; §4's indeterminate path does have the Limiter reconcile afterward, but the adapter gives it no signal that a reconciliation is owed, so this belongs with D1.20's consumer obligation. (b) `flatten(symbol)` on an unheld symbol returns None, places nothing, raises nothing and logs nothing, so on the protective path "already flat" and "the mirror has lost this position" are indistinguishable — and the adapter holds `_mirror_stale`, which already knows which world it may be in; contrast the per-symbol failure path a few lines below in the same method, which collects and raises with the full picture (`debug.md` failure mode #11 / doctrine C.7). (c) `disconnect()` emits `on_session(DOWN)` on every call including when there was never a session, while `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §2A defines `on_session` as connectivity **transitions** — fails toward halted, so characterisation rather than alarm, and it pairs with the edge-versus-level distinction already recorded for `_mirror_stale` | broker-order |
| D1.29 | **`Balance.cash`, `Balance.maint_margin` and `Balance.init_margin` carry two different meanings depending on which writer wrote last** | ARC 014/017 code, found ARC 020 (A8, the per-writer field-meaning rule) | unassigned — adapter-and-seam decision with a consumer half, so it discharges with R2 or with a seam change R2 has agreed to. `query_balance` populates all four figures from `accountSummaryAsync`. `_on_ib_account_value` — the **push** path, which is what `on_balance` actually delivers — writes `0.0` for `cash`, `maint_margin` and `init_margin`, because it fires on the `NetLiquidation` tag and has nothing else to report. So a consumer reading `on_balance(...).cash` receives `0.0`, which is not a missing value but a **confident lie**, and it is a lie in precisely the shape of the two `avg_price` incidents: both writers are individually plausible, and only a new *class* of test could expose the disagreement. §14 makes this load-bearing rather than cosmetic — *"Survival is watched on net-liq; sizing is computed on cash. Never conflate."* A Limiter sizing on a pushed `cash` of `0.0` computes a size of zero, or divides by it. **Deliberately not repaired in ARC 020**, because both candidate fixes — `Optional[float]` per figure, or a `partial: bool` on `Balance` — are seam changes whose consumer half is R2's, and the standing rule for this arc was enumerate, assert, report, not resolve. **It is PINNED by assertion**: the current per-writer meanings are asserted per writer, so the day a writer starts populating those fields the suite reddens and the decision is forced rather than made silently. Discharge is a decision about whether the seam distinguishes "not reported" from "zero", not a one-line fix | broker-order |

What is missing is the drive: take the ticks a real feed delivers through `on_tick` (or the polled history `subscribe` falls back to at Stage 0), publish a bar, poll again with a genuinely different value, assert the published bar unchanged field-by-field and the revision observable — with the non-vacuity assertion that the second poll really differed, since a re-poll returning identical data proves nothing. This arc added two more as flagged Nix additions because at Stage 0 the tick firehose does not exist (GAP-D1) and D1.14's revision has nowhere else to surface. A port change binds EVERY vendor, so this is an architect decision and not an adapter's — which is why it is a row and not a patch | broker-seam |

## D2 — Doctrine rules Nix does not yet satisfy

From `nix_check_contract.md` §15.3.

| D2.13 | §7.12 / D.6 | **discharged ARC 018** — the `pytest-affected` entry is no longer bare `pytest --testmon`; it is a runtime-gate program that makes the state of `.testmondata` an input to the verdict and prints `SELECTED=` on every run. Zero selection is no longer terminal: it is named (`NOTHING-SELECTED`, `SCOPE-BLIND`, `SELECTOR-BROKEN`, all non-zero exits, exit 2 kept distinct from exit 1 per B.2) and then escalated to a non-incremental run so the commit is measured rather than waved through. Proven in order on this tree: **non-vacuity** (cold database → `collected 159 items`); **defect reproduced pre-fix** (warm, no change → `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / `no tests ran` / hook **Passed** / exit 0); **fix under identical conditions** (hook **Failed**, exit code 2, `RUNTIME-GATE verdict: NOTHING-SELECTED`; with escalation on, `mode=full-escalated(zero-selection) SELECTED=159 MEASURED-PASS` in 11.6 s); **D3.4's selection proof re-run intact** (plant `assert 1 == 2` → `collected 9 items`, neither 0 nor the whole suite, `FAILED …test_arc018_plant_testmon` naming `scripts/tests/test_check_venv.py:214`, exit 1 → restored to sha256 `fd5d4992…877b5` → `collected 8 items`, pass). `__pycache__` purged between every step. **Two findings came out of building it, and both are worse than the row as written.** (i) A tracked source file can change and select **nothing**: `scripts/nixverify/__init__.py` is in scope for every other hook and has **no fingerprint at all** in testmon's graph, so appending a line to it produced `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / exit 0 — the old gate was green over a real, staged, tracked change. (ii) testmon does not notice its own corrupted record: overwriting one `file_fp.fsha` with zeroes still produced `changed files: 0` / `collected 0 items` / exit 0. The new gate catches both, the second by recomputing the git-blob hashes testmon stores rather than by asking testmon what changed. **Residual, stated rather than discovered later:** `.testmondata` is still untracked and still per-machine — tracking it was rejected (binary SQLite plus WAL sidecars is a second source of truth that goes stale on write; ARC 016 rejected the same shape for `downloads/*.py`), so what changed is that its state is now visible and load-bearing, not that it became reviewable. See **D2.16** for the debt this repair itself opened. **PHASE 4 CORRECTION — the first design of this fix was wrong, and integration found it.** `SELECTOR-BROKEN` originally *terminated* on `drift and selected == 0`. Reproduction is one line: append a comment to any test file and run the gate twice. Both runs print `SELECTOR-BROKEN … exit 1`, identically — it does **not** self-clear, so a commit touching only comments or docstrings was permanently red with a verdict naming a selector that was working correctly. The cause is a real asymmetry rather than a bug in either tool: this gate's corroboration is **content-based** (a git blob hash), while testmon's selection is **semantic** (per-method checksums), so a behaviour-neutral edit legitimately changes the content hash while changing no method checksum. Content drift is evidence the record is *stale*; that is not the same claim as *the selector is broken*, and the original arm conflated them. Repaired by escalating instead of terminating: the full run measures everything, so a subsequent pass is honest, `drift=` still names every affected file on the scope line, and the arm stays terminal under `NIX_RUNTIME_GATE=noescalate` — which is how the A2-ii corrupted-`fsha` demonstration was taken in the first place, so that evidence is unaffected. Re-proved after the repair: comment-only edit → `mode=full-escalated(SELECTOR-BROKEN:…) SELECTED=180 → MEASURED-PASS exit 0`, and the next run shows `drift=0` — it self-clears. Can-fail re-proved on the final gate: plant → `SELECTED=9` (neither 0 nor 181) → `FAIL` naming the site → restore to sha256 `fd5d4992…` → `SELECTED=8` → `MEASURED-PASS`. **This is the argument for D2.16's discharge: the defect was invisible while the program lived in a YAML string and surfaced within minutes of it becoming testable code** | — | hooks |
| D2.14 | `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §2A:71 / §4:241 / §12A:830 · doctrine B.4 | **A hand-rolled retry loop on the order path is banned and undetected.** `checks/check_order_path_bans.py` (ARC 017) proves *no retry **library*** (`tenacity`/`backoff`/`retrying`) and *no loop-blocking **call***; it does **not** prove nothing retries. `for _ in range(3): self.place_order(...)` passes both arms. §2.1 exists because a retry on the order path turns one intended order into two, and that is a semantic property, not an import. A PASS from that gate must be read as exactly what it measures — the gate's own docstring says so under §7.12 condition 6, and this row is the ledger half of that admission. Discharge needs a semantic instrument (call-graph reachability from `place_order` back to itself, or an AST loop-containing-a-send analysis), which is a different and harder gate. **CITATION CORRECTED ARC 018 — this row cited §2.1 of the frozen spec and there is no §2.1.** Headings run `## 2.` → `## 2A.`; the only `x.1` headings in the file are 12.1/12.10/12.11. “§2.1” was the ARC 017 *brief's* prohibition 1 — a task document — propagated into this row and into `check_order_path_bans.py`'s own docstring. The ban is real; the anchor was not. Real anchors, all verified on disk: §2A:71 `query_order_status` — “pending-timeout resolution (**never auto-resend**)”; §4:241 “issues order-status query, **never auto-resends**”; §12A:830 `PENDING_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` — “status query, **never resend**”. **NARROWED ARC 018, NOT DISCHARGED.** `check_order_path_bans` arm (iii) now detects three retry shapes structurally by AST — `loop_contains_send`, `bounded_counter_send`, `except_reinvokes_send` — with can-fail proven per shape and a per-site `(file, qualname, shape, verb)` suppression registry that self-expires. Measured false-positive rate on the merged tree: **1 hit over 9 loops in 4 files**, and that one hit (`IBKRBrokerOrder.flatten`'s per-symbol fan-out) is a site a reviewer should see. **Three residuals keep this row open, each named beside the gate:** retry by *recursion* (`except: return self._send(n-1)` — fires no shape); indirection deeper than one hop (`INDIRECTION_DEPTH = 1`); and retry across a thread or process boundary, which no scanner can see. | D2.16 | C.10 / §7.12 / #14 | **The runtime gate's own program is outside every gate.** Opened by ARC 018's own repair, stated rather than left to be found. The `pytest-affected` verdict logic — database audit, independent hash recomputation, verdict taxonomy, escalation — is a Python program living inside the `entry:` string of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. `ruff`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit` and `complexipy` all see a YAML file; none of them see that Python, and no pytest test drives it. So the instrument that decides whether the runtime was measured is the one artifact in the tree with no static check and no test over it, which is failure mode #14 aimed at the gate itself. It is there because ARC 018 scoped sub-agent A's writes to `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `pyproject.toml`, `scratch/**` and this ledger — `scripts/` was forbidden, and a gate that must exist somewhere ends up wherever it is allowed. Discharge: lift the program verbatim into a tracked `scripts/runtime_gate.py`, point `entry:` at it, and give it tests — at which point every hook covers it and the §7.12 answer 7 in the config header can be deleted rather than reworded. **DISCHARGED ARC 018 Phase 4, by the parent, which owns `scripts/`.** The program was lifted verbatim to `scripts/runtime_gate.py` and `entry:` now reads `./.venv/bin/python scripts/runtime_gate.py`. It is covered by all eight hooks (ruff-check, ruff-format, pylint, mypy, bandit, complexipy and the runtime gate itself) and by `scripts/tests/test_runtime_gate.py`, whose first assertion is a non-vacuity check deriving the invoked path **out of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`** so the suite cannot drift onto an orphan module. **The lift paid for itself immediately:** static analysis of the now-visible code produced bandit B405/B314/B607 and pylint C0209/R0913 findings that the YAML string had hidden, and integration testing of the now-testable code found the `SELECTOR-BROKEN` false positive recorded under D2.13. **COUNT CORRECTED ARC 023 (B2), MEASURED NOT READ: it is 7 of 8 hooks, not 8.** pre-commit's own `Classifier` over `git.get_all_files()` selects `scripts/runtime_gate.py` for `ruff-check`, `ruff-format`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit (production)` and `complexipy`, plus `pytest-affected` which is `always_run` — **`bandit (tests)` does not select it**, because its `files: ^scripts/tests/` excludes everything outside the test tree. The sentence above names SEVEN hooks in its own parenthetical while claiming eight, so it contradicted itself where it stood. **This is D3.1's defect verbatim, eight arcs later** — a coverage claim recorded against two bandit instruments when only one of them can see the file — and it is why B.7's derive-never-restate applies to coverage claims and not only to numbers. The substantive control is unaffected: six static hooks plus the companion test genuinely read the module, and `scripts/tests/test_runtime_gate.py` genuinely derives its import target out of `.pre-commit-config.yaml`. The discharge stands; the number did not | discharged ARC 018 | hooks |
| D2.15 | C.10 / §7.4 | **`check_order_path_bans` scans one directory, and nothing guards that the order path still lives there.** Scope derives at run time from `ORDER_PATH_DIRS = ("scripts/broker",)` via `rglob`, so a *new file* under `scripts/broker/` is covered automatically — the moving-value trap the gate was written to avoid. But a new *home* is not: an adapter added at `scripts/risk/` or `scripts/limiter/` is silently outside the scan while both required members remain present and non-vacuity still passes, so the gate stays green having never looked. §7.12 condition 4 beside the gate, unguarded and named there. This is the same shape as D2.11 (no owner for shared global measurements) but concrete. Discharge with whichever arc first puts order-path code outside `scripts/broker/`, which is also the moment it becomes discoverable. **NARROWED ARC 018, NOT DISCHARGED.** Scope no longer comes from a path anyone typed: the gate derives it from **file content** — every directory holding a module that declares the order port (defines `ORDER_PORT_VERBS`, or a class carrying ≥ 4 roster-verb methods), read from each file's own AST, union'd with `ORDER_PATH_DIRS` as a floor so the scan can never collapse *below* its historical scope. The name `ORDER_PATH_DIRS` is deliberately kept so this row's own citation does not go stale in the act of discharging it.
