| D2.17 | C.10 / §7.4 / #14 | **An UNATTRIBUTED `§`-citation is the defect that produced §2.1, and `check_spec_citations` treats it as an advisory.** Opened ARC 019 by the gate that closed the attributed half, stated rather than left to be found. The gate resolves a citation only when the surrounding block names the document; a citation naming no document is counted, printed on every run, and never a violation. But "banned by §2.1", with no document, is *exactly* how a task brief's own prohibition acquired frozen-spec authority — the reader supplies the document. **Measured ARC 019 on the merged tree: 1144 citations scanned, 136 attributed, 958 unattributed.** So the class this gate was built for is, today, ~7× larger outside its judgement than inside it. Discharge: promote unattributed to a violation **inside `GOVERNED_ROOTS` first**, after the citations there carry their document — the repair is to write the filename beside the citation, at which point the existing gate starts checking it with no edit to the gate. | D2.18 | B.4 / §7.4 | **Citation attribution is a proximity heuristic, so the gate proves a citation is not DANGLING and cannot prove it is APT.** Opened ARC 019 beside D2.17 because it is the other half of the same limit and folding them together would make one look covered by the other's discharge. `§13` is a real heading in four of the five indexed documents; which one a given `§13` means is decided by the nearest document alias within the enclosing structural block, bounded at 160 characters. That calibration was measured — an unbounded block window misattributed `check_order_path_bans.py`'s standing-question banner to `nix_check_contract.md` — but a heuristic that is right on this tree today is not a proof. A citation pointing at a real section of the wrong document passes. Closing it needs either an explicit citation syntax (a document token in every citation, which is a tree-wide prose change) or semantic matching of the cited text against the section, which is reading intent out of prose. Neither trade is made here; both are named. Discharge with D2.17, whose explicit-attribution repair is also this row's repair | verify |
| D2.19 | C.10 / §7.4 / #14 | **The broker-datafeed depth claim's vocabulary has no module-basename half, so it selects on roster prose alone.** Opened ARC 021 (B3) by the claim that created the limit, stated rather than left to be found. `broker_order_open_debt_rows` scopes a row two ways — an order-path module basename, or an order roster identifier. Its datafeed counterpart `broker_datafeed_open_debt_rows` has only the second: the sole class implementing the datafeed port outside the seam lives in `ibkr_mapping.py`, which implements the order port too, so counting that basename would report order rows as datafeed debt. The vocabulary is therefore five distinctive identifiers (`subscribe`, `unsubscribe`, `feed_lag`, `on_tick`, `on_feed_status` — the datafeed roster minus the shared `connect`/`disconnect`) and nothing else, which makes the ARC 020 D1.30/D1.31 trap **sharper here than on the order side**: a row about the feed that never spells one of those five words is invisible to the claim, and no diff to the claim ever appears. The set self-heals the moment a module implements the datafeed port and not the order port — the derivation already subtracts — so this row discharges by the adapter landing, and until then the only mitigation is that every selected row id is printed on every run and a new row is checked by RUNNING the claim, never by reading the row. **THE CONTAMINATION RUNS THE OTHER WAY TOO, AND ARC 021 MEASURED IT RATHER THAN INFERRING IT.** Adding these datafeed rows moved `broker_order_open_debt_rows` from 11 to 13, and neither new selection contains a single order roster identifier: both were picked up by the ORDER claim's module-basename half, because they name `ibkr_mapping.py` and that file is globbed out of `ORDER_PATH_DIRS`. One module hosts both §2A adapters, so a row about the datafeed half of it is counted as broker-order depth. That is not a defect in either claim's arithmetic — it is the module-basename rule being unable to say which library a shared file's row is about — and it is disclosed here because the alternative is a broker-order depth figure that rises when nobody touched broker-order. It resolves the same way this row does: when the datafeed gets a module of its own, the shared basename stops being the only way to name the subject. **NARROWED ARC 022 (C3), NOT DISCHARGED — AND THE PREDICTION IN THIS ROW'S OWN TEXT WAS HALF WRONG, WHICH IS THE FINDING.** This row says the set *"self-heals the moment a module implements the datafeed port and not the order port"*. ARC 021 landed exactly that module. **The datafeed side did self-heal**: `_datafeed_only_modules` now returns `['broker_datafeed_ibkr.py']` with no edit, and that basename is a vocabulary term for `broker_datafeed_open_debt_rows` today. **The order side did not, and got worse.** The order claim's basename half was the raw directory glob under `ORDER_PATH_DIRS`, so `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py` — which lives under `scripts/broker` because that is where the second library's code lives — became an ORDER vocabulary term the moment it was written, and three rows carrying **no broker-order roster identifier at all** (D1.13, D3.9, D3.10) were counted as broker-order depth. Landing the dedicated module did not remove a contamination source; it added one. **REPAIR, and it is the mirror of the rule this claim's counterpart has always had:** clause (i) of the order-side scoping rule now reads *the basename of a module under the order path AND not implementing the broker-datafeed port*. The subtraction is read out of the seam's own `DATAFEED_PORT_VERBS`, so a module joins or leaves by being written; nothing is listed by hand. **MEASURED, OLD RULE versus NEW RULE, over three trees, so the correction can be told apart from the work:** ARC 020 tree 11 versus **11**, with the identical eleven-row selection — the repair does not retroactively rewrite banked history; ARC 021 tree 16 versus **13**; ARC 022 tree 16 versus **13**. **The ARC 021 series entry of 11 -> 16 was therefore +3 CONTAMINATION and +2 WORK, and the corrected series is 11 -> 13.** Can-fail with control, in a throwaway copy: a planted datafeed-only module plus a planted order-only module, each named by one planted ledger row — the old rule selected **both** planted rows (16 -> 18), the new rule selects **only the order-only one** (13 -> 14), so the subtraction discriminates rather than merely subtracting. A non-vacuity floor was added with it and demonstrated: planting three datafeed verbs into `IBKRBrokerOrder` makes every order module look like a datafeed module, and the claim now raises `order-path vocabulary [...] holds no module implementing the order port` — CANNOT-MEASURE — where the old rule silently returned 16. **WHAT KEEPS THIS ROW OPEN, and it is a residual this repair does not touch: the ROSTER half is still not distinctive.** `connect` and `disconnect` are in both rosters. The datafeed claim subtracts them; the order claim cannot, because they are genuinely broker-order verbs and an order row about connecting is real order debt. **Measured false positive on this tree: D1.38, whose entire subject is the datafeed port's sync/async split, is selected as broker-order depth on the single word `connect`.** Both mechanical repairs are worse than the disease — dropping the shared verbs blinds the order claim to every real connect/disconnect row, and demanding two roster hits blinds it to single-verb rows — so the false positive is disclosed and left. A second residual is created by the repair and is stated rather than discovered later: a row genuinely about `ibkr_mapping.py`'s ORDER half that names no order roster identifier is now invisible to the order claim, because a shared-host basename no longer attributes on its own. **SWEEP RESULT ARC 023 (B2): THIS ROW'S MITIGATION ACTUALLY MEASURES.** *"every selected row id is printed on every run"* was re-executed rather than read — both depth probes print their whole selection and their whole vocabulary to stderr on every run (`selected: D1.17, D1.19, …, D3.8` on the order side, `selected: D1.13, D1.14, …, D1.39` on the datafeed side). Weak by design, and real. **SERIES CORRECTION ARC 023 (B), RE-DERIVED AGAINST ALL THREE TREES WITH TODAY'S RULE — MEASURED, NOT READ OUT OF THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE.** Method, so it is reproducible and so it is clear no worktree was created while a sibling agent held the repository: each tree was extracted read-only with `git archive <rev> | tar -x`, and **this** tree's probe was pointed at it with `--nix-home`, so one rule ran over three ledgers. `436933e` (ARC 020) = **11** over 40 open rows; `842edb5` (ARC 021) = **13** over 53; `df36405` (ARC 022) = **13** over 62; this branch after B's edits = **13** over 62. **ARC 022's delta on this claim was therefore ZERO.** ARC 022's own results reported *"level 13, Δ +2 this arc"*, which is the corrected ARC 020 to ARC 021 transition attached to the wrong arc — a real number annotating the wrong interval, which is `debug.md` §7.4's stale anchor in its subtlest form, because both the level and the delta are individually true and the pairing is not. Nothing in this ledger stated that delta, so nothing here needed unwinding; the correction is recorded because the figure is banked outside this file | verify |
| D2.20 | C.10 / §7.4 | **`check_datafeed_granted_mode` arm B3 is name-identity, not dataflow.** Opened ARC 021 Phase 4 by the repair that created the limit, stated rather than left to be found. B3 asks whether the value handed to the vendor's mode request is the value reported as granted, and answers by intersecting two sets of NAMES. Phase 4 excluded `self` and `cls` because every method call contributes the receiver to both sets, which made the arm redden a correct adapter. The residual is that a grant laundered through a rename is caught only when the rename shares a name: `m = self._requested_mode; ... granted_mode=m` is caught, an unrelated-looking attribute is not. **MEASURED, not theorised** — a plant substituting `requested_mode` for `granted_mode` inside the adapter-wide accessor passed this arm. Arm B1's behavioural drive is the compensating control, and the pytest pair added in Phase 4 is what actually fails that plant. **ASSESSED ARC 022 (C4) — CLOSURE REFUSED, WITH THE COST ARGUED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN.** The refusal follows ARC 020's D3.7 standard: a named gap is worth more than a weak closure. **What closing it would actually require.** B3's question is *does the value reported as granted derive from the value requested*. That is a dataflow question and the current answer is set intersection over identifiers. Making it dataflow needs reaching-definitions that are (a) **field-sensitive** — the real adapter parks the grant in per-symbol state, so `self._symbols[symbol].granted` must be distinguishable from `self._requested_mode`; (b) **inter-procedural** — the write happens in a vendor callback and the read happens in an accessor, two methods that never appear in one another's AST; and (c) **callback-entry aware** — the value enters as a parameter of a function the adapter never calls itself, so no call site connects the definition to the use. That is a static analyser, not a gate arm, and this project has already refused this exact trade twice on the same grounds: D2.15 refused call-site derivation because it multiplies the false-positive surface by the size of the code it would have to read, and D2.18 refused semantic matching of citation text because it means reading intent out of prose. Building it here would put a third such engine in a boot-time check that must stay inside its measured 0.4 s. **The cheap approximations were considered and each is a doctrine B.4 hazard rather than a saving.** Widening `_granted_names` beyond the `granted_mode=` keyword to any attribute whose name contains `grant` is a spelling rule wearing a correctness gate's exit code, and it reddens on the first adapter that calls the field something else. Requiring the granted value to come from a vendor callback parameter is a shape rule keyed to *this* vendor, which is the D3.10 defect being manufactured a second time. **A MEASUREMENT TAKEN WHILE ASSESSING, WHICH STRENGTHENS THE REFUSAL AND IS WHY IT IS RECORDED HERE:** run against today's tree, arm B3 reports `request-call names ['_requested_mode', 'value']` for the real adapter and its `granted` side is **empty**, because the real adapter has no `granted_mode=` keyword anywhere — the grant arrives as a callback parameter into per-symbol state. So on its real subject this arm is not merely approximate, it is **vacuous**, which is D3.10 restated in the arm's own output. The residual is therefore larger than this row said, and the honest disposition is to leave it open and say so rather than to close it on an approximation. **SWEEP RESULT ARC 023 (B2): THIS ROW'S FIRST COMPENSATING CONTROL DOES NOT MEASURE.** *"Arm B1's behavioural drive is the compensating control"* — arm B1 is D3.16's subject and it has never once driven the adapter's `granted_mode`. Re-measured on today's tree, all three legs return `AttributeError: 'int' object has no attribute '_symbols'` and the gate is CANNOT_MEASURE. The SECOND control this row names — the ARC 021 Phase 4 pytest pair — was checked and **does** measure: both cases exist, drive the real adapter, and carry their plant in their own docstrings. So the residual is compensated by a test and not by the gate, which is materially weaker than the row read, and D3.10 is the reason that distinction matters. **THE AIM QUESTION, ASKED OF THIS ROW IN ARC 023 (S2) AND ANSWERED YES — WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF D2.21's ANSWER AND IS WORTH RECORDING FOR THAT REASON.** D3.15's sweep established that a compensating control must be checked for AIM before EXISTENCE. This row's first control now EXISTS (arm B1 rebuilt, D3.16), so the aim question is live: *would a working arm B1 actually cover a grant laundered through an unrelated-looking attribute — the residual B3 cannot see?* **YES, and it is measured rather than argued.** Arm B1 is behavioural: it subscribes with no grant callback and asserts the reported mode is the sentinel, so ANY derivation of the reported value from the requested one — by rename, by attribute, by anything — reports a mode where the sentinel is owed and fails. ARC 021's plant 2 is exactly that laundering and it is now CAUGHT, naming `granted_mode()@subscribed-ungranted` and `granted_mode()@divergent-grants`; so is P3, which launders it one level deeper inside the grant callback. Arm B1 does not read names at all, which is precisely why it reaches what B3's name identity cannot. **THE ROW STAYS OPEN AND THE REFUSAL STANDS.** B3 is still name-identity, its granted-side set is still EMPTY on this adapter, and this arc did not build the field-sensitive inter-procedural dataflow the ARC 022 assessment costed. What changed is that the residual is now compensated by an instrument that measures AND is aimed at it, instead of by a sentence naming one that never ran | unassigned — needs field-sensitive inter-procedural dataflow, refused ARC 022, compensating control real since ARC 023 | verify |
| D2.21 | C.10 / §7.12 | **`check_datafeed_bar_seal` arm 2 does not check guard POLARITY.** Opened ARC 021 Phase 4 alongside the repair that taught the arm a second guard spelling. The arm now recognises both the membership form and the lookup-then-sentinel form, and treats a store as guarded if either dominates it — but it does not distinguish `if found is None` from `if found is not None`, exactly as it never distinguished `in` from `not in` (the gate's own §7.12 condition 5). An inverted guard would overwrite every sealed entry and only ever store on the re-poll. The residual is UNCHANGED IN SIZE by the repair rather than introduced by it, which is the reason it is recorded here rather than treated as new damage. Arm 4's behavioural drive is the compensating control. **DISCHARGED ARC 022 (C4), AND THE COMPENSATING CONTROL THIS ROW LEANED ON TURNED OUT NOT TO EXIST — SEE D3.15.** `_guard_keys` is replaced by `_absent_proofs`, which answers a test with TWO sets instead of one: what it proves ABSENT when true, and what it proves absent when false. The `If` handler applies the first to `body` and the second to `orelse`, so a seal is credited only on the branch where the key is known not to be there. **WHY IT CANNOT BE A WEAKENING, STRUCTURALLY AND NOT BY ASSERTION:** every branch now receives a strict SUBSET of what it received before — `body` went from `In ∪ NotIn ∪ Is ∪ IsNot` to `NotIn ∪ Is`, `orelse` to `In ∪ IsNot` — so nothing that failed before can pass now. **MEASURED, eight guard spellings in a throwaway copy of the tree, `__pycache__` purged between every step, plus the real adapter as the control. Before → after:** correct membership `if key not in store: store[k]=v` PASS→PASS; correct sentinel `found=store.get(k); if found is None:` PASS→PASS; correct else-branch `if found is not None: revise() else: store[k]=v` PASS→PASS; unguarded store **FAIL→FAIL**, still naming its site (this is ARC 021's plant P4, re-run intact); guarded-but-emits-no-event **FAIL→FAIL** (arm 3 untouched); `if key in store: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL**; `if found is not None: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL** — that is this row's subject verbatim; and `if key not in store or self._force: store[k]=v` **PASS→FAIL**. **The last is a residual this row did not name and that building the table found rather than reasoning produced it:** a disjunction weakens the proof to nothing, and the old walk-every-`Compare` reader counted it as a guard because it never looked at how the tests were combined. **DOCTRINE B.4's OTHER EDGE, and this gate has already fallen off it once** — it reddened the correct implementation of its own subject on the first adapter it bound to. So the real adapter was re-run as a CONTROL after the change and its output is **byte-identical** to the output before it, exit 0 either way; arms 1 and 4 are untouched, the blast radius being one function and one branch read only by arms 2 and 3. **WHAT THIS EXPLICITLY DOES NOT DO: it does not bind the gate.** A stricter arm is still an arm that has never failed the real adapter. D3.9 and D3.10 are unaffected and `check_datafeed_bar_seal` remains UNBOUND in the ARC 022 census. **SWEEP RESULT ARC 023 (B2) — THE CONTROL WAS MIS-AIMED AS WELL AS ABSENT, AND THAT IS THE NEW PART.** D3.15 established that arm 4 measures nothing. The sweep asked the prior question — *would a working arm 4 have covered this residual?* — and the answer is **no**. This row's subject is guard POLARITY in the SOURCE (`if found is not None: store[k]=v`); arm 4 constructs published TYPES and drives immutability on them, a path an inverted guard in `_ingest_history` never reaches. **The row is NOT reopened**, and the argument is in the sweep section above: the ARC 022 discharge rests on the `_absent_proofs` strict-subset repair and its eight-spelling table, not on arm 4, and reopening it would make this ledger assert an unrepaired residual the measurement says is repaired. **RE-VERIFIED ARC 023 (S2) AND THE VERIFICATION UPGRADED THE EVIDENCE.** Two things were checked rather than assumed. (1) **The aim finding survives the existence finding.** Arm 4 now measures (D3.15 discharged), so the sweep's second claim was re-asked with a working arm — and it holds: arm 4 drives published TYPES and an inverted guard in `_ingest_history` is still on no path it executes. A control becoming real does not make it competent, which is the sweep's lesson surviving its own repair. The row is therefore still NOT reopened, on the argument already recorded above. (2) **The polarity detection was re-taken against the REAL adapter, which ARC 022 never did.** That arc's eight-spelling table was over synthetic spellings with the real adapter as a PASS control; here the plant lands in `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:_ingest_history` itself — `if sealed is None:` inverted to `if sealed is not None:` — and the gate returns exit 1 naming `scripts/broker/broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:_ingest_history:1704`, control exit 0 either side, restore byte-identical by sha256. Under the rule of record at the head of this file that moves this property from demonstrated-against-a-fake to demonstrated-against-the-subject | discharged ARC 022 | verify |

Sub-agent A hit the consequence live: a `@pytest.mark.parametrize` over a non-literal turned `pytest_collected_tests` from a live comparison into NOT MEASURED, and it was caught only because A ran the check by hand. | D2.23 | C.2 / #12 | **`check_derived_claims` silently ignores a path argument, so a scratch-tree measurement can be a real-tree measurement wearing a scratch tree's name.** Opened ARC 022 (C2) by the plant that hit it, stated rather than left to be found. The gate resolves its home from its own `__file__` — which is correct and is the non-restating spelling every other derivation in it uses — but its `__main__` block reads `sys.argv` only for `--probe`, so any other argument is discarded without a word. Every sibling gate in this registry (`check_order_path_bans`, `check_spec_citations`, `check_datafeed_granted_mode`, `check_datafeed_bar_seal`) takes a home path as an argument, so the convention invites exactly the mistake. **MEASURED, and it is why this row exists rather than a note:** the ARC 022 census planted the banked series figure 53 -> 47 in a throwaway copy of the tree and ran `check_derived_claims.py <scratch>` from this worktree. It printed `check_debt_open_items=53 [derived:ledger_rows=53, stated:series_table_latest_row=53]` and **exit 0** — a serene green over a planted defect, because it had measured a different tree entirely. The plant only fired when re-taken through the scratch tree's own copy of the gate. That is `debug.md` failure mode #12 — a proof taken in one environment presented as a claim about another — with the twist that the instrument names no environment at all, so nothing in the output distinguishes the two runs. Discharge: either accept a home argument like every sibling, or **refuse an unrecognised argument loudly** — the second is stricter, is three lines, and is the one doctrine C.7 asks for. Not done here because this gate is the tree's most heavily-consumed instrument and both sibling agents were live in adjacent worktrees when it was found | unassigned | verify |

### Promoted to doctrine — ARC 016

The recurring failure class **"a green light that measured nothing"** was promoted out of this ledger
into `debug.md` **§7.12** (doctrine v1.2.0), as a standing question required of every new gate —
***what would have to be true for this to pass while measuring nothing?*** — answered **in writing, at
the point the gate is built**. Failure mode **#14** (*scope set by an external mutable list*) was added
to the §8 catalogue. The seven-instance evidence base is tabulated in §7.12 and is not restated here.

> **Citation correction.** The ARC 016 brief directed this promotion at debt item **D2.8**. D2.8 is
> doctrine **B.7** — *no harness parses a constant out of a document and asserts the code equals it* —
> which is the **derive-never-restate** class, not the vacuous-pass class, and it remains **open and
> unassigned**. Nothing about it was discharged by the promotion. The items in this ledger that
> actually carry the promoted class are **D1.10** (each hook actually capable of failing), **D2.7**
> (nothing baselined on a pristine tree — bandit scanning nothing is its measured instance), **D2.12**
> (a suite that silently skips a gate reports GREEN) and the whole of **D3**, whose header states the
> principle outright. Those are the items §7.12 now stands over.
>
> Recorded rather than silently redirected, because a pointer that reads as authoritative while
> naming the wrong target is a **stale literal anchor** — `debug.md` §7.4, and the same class the
> ledger's own miscounted series row (instance #2 in §7.12) belongs to. Left uncorrected it would
> have produced a future arc "discharging D2.8" by writing a doctrine section that has nothing to do
> with parsing constants out of documents.

## D3 — Instruments whose can-fail has never been demonstrated

Doctrine C.2: a gate is guilty until shown able to say no.

| D3.7 | **the PINNED hook environment itself** — every `rev:` in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` | **opened ARC 019, open.** Split out of D1.10 rather than folded into `check_hook_suite`'s discharge, because it is a different property and folding it in would let that gate's green be read as covering it. D1.10 asked whether "the pinned `rev:` moves to an environment that scans nothing" is checked. `check_hook_suite` arm (4) answers half of it: the environment each hook resolves to *is* the store row keyed to the pinned rev, and every other resident environment for the same repo is named on every run — measured ARC 019, the store holds bandit at both `1.8.6` and `1.9.4` and the hooks resolve to `1.9.4`. The half it cannot answer is whether the **pinned** environment is non-vacuous. Nothing structural distinguishes a bandit that scans 21 files from one that raises on all 21 and exits 0; that is the ARC 006 defect verbatim, and the only thing that ever detected it was running the tool against a known-bad input. So this row is the D3 question asked of the *environment* rather than of the hook entry: for each pinned rev, drive a canary the hook must reject and observe it rejected. D3.1–D3.6 each did that once, by hand, at one rev; what is owed is the same demonstration re-taken whenever a pin moves, which is a bump-time obligation and not a boot-time one. Until then a pin remains a declaration, and `check_hook_suite`'s docstring says so where the gate is defined. **ASSESSED ARC 020 (C3) — REFUSED, WITH THE COST MEASURED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN.** The brief asked whether a per-hook canary is workable or worse than the debt. It was built and run, against a **throwaway repository** in a scratch directory — never `~/nix/.git/hooks`, which is shared by every linked worktree and would have disarmed a sibling agent mid-arc — carrying a copy of the config, a copy of `pyproject.toml`, and two planted fixtures. **The mechanism works and the runtime cost is not the objection:** `pre-commit run --all-files` over the pinned suite took **1.55 s wall**, against `check_hook_suite`'s ~1.5 s, so the cheap-enough argument is settled in the canary's favour and is not the reason this is refused. What the run measured instead is four costs, three of them structural: **(1) two generic fixtures caught only five of seven pinned hooks** — `ruff-check`, `pylint`, `mypy`, `bandit (production)` and `bandit (tests)` all Failed and named their site, while `ruff-format` and `complexipy` **Passed**, because neither fixture carried a defect they detect. A real canary therefore needs a fixture **per hook**, hand-authored against what each tool is believed to catch, which is a hand-maintained rubric of tool behaviour and goes stale silently the moment a pin moves a rule between preview and stable. That is the stale-anchor class this apparatus exists to remove, rebuilt inside the instrument built to remove it, and it is the same objection that made ARC 020 refuse a per-element confidence dimension one gate over. **(2) The canary repairs itself out of existence.** `ruff-check` runs with `--fix`, and the planted file's sha256 went `f7d415d0…f880f9` before the run to `ab4b045c…6e8de9` after it — the suite silently edited the evidence. So no static tracked fixture can serve; it must be re-materialised on every run, which is workable in a throwaway and impossible in the tree. **(3) The in-tree variant is blocked outright.** A fixture living in the real tree makes every `pre-commit run --all-files` permanently RED unless it is hidden by an `exclude:` in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` — and an `exclude:` that hides the canary from the real run is a scope narrowing indistinguishable from failure mode #14, which is the class the canary was supposed to close. That file is also outside sub-agent C's write scope, so the variant could not have been landed here in any case. **(4) The throwaway variant proves the pinned tool non-vacuous under a RECONSTRUCTED configuration, not the live one.** The copy must carry every config the hooks reference — `bandit` is invoked `-c pyproject.toml`, and `ruff`, `pylint` and `mypy` all read settings from that file. A copy that drifts measures a different tool configuration than the one that guards commits, which is failure mode #12 (a proof taken in one environment presented as a claim about another), and deriving the copy list rather than typing it reintroduces #14 inside the fix for #14. **One further finding, recorded so a future harness does not claim coverage it cannot have:** `pytest-affected` is `repo: local`, `language: system`, and carries **no `rev:` at all**, so it has no pinned environment to canary. In the throwaway it reported Failed for an environment reason (`./.venv/bin/python` not found), not because it caught a plant. A per-hook canary is structurally **seven of eight**, consistent with this row's subject being `rev:` pins, and any instrument built later must say so rather than let its green read as suite-wide. **WHAT WOULD MAKE THIS CLOSEABLE, and it is a different shape from a gate.** This row's own text already says the obligation is **bump-time, not boot-time**, and the measurement supports that: the property changes only when a `rev:` moves, so a canary run at boot and weekly buys nothing between bumps and actively converts a bump-time review into a background green nobody reads. What is missing is not the canary; it is the **trigger**. A bump-time trigger needs a banked fingerprint of the pinned revs and something that compares against it at an arc boundary — which is **D2.4** (nothing runs `verify.py` at an arc boundary and banks its verdict; Nix has no `bank.sh`), with **D2.5** (no known-red marker) beside it. **D3.7 is therefore downstream of D2.4, which was not previously recorded anywhere**, and that dependency is this assessment's main product. Closeable when three things hold together: D2.4 exists so a pin change is detectable as a change; one arc holds write scope over both `checks/**` and `.pre-commit-config.yaml`; and the fixture set can be derived from something the tools own rather than authored from what a person believes they catch — no tool in this suite ships such a corpus in a pinned, addressable form today | unassigned — bump-time, not boot-time: discharges with whichever arc next moves a `rev:` pin, and is blocked behind D2.4 for the trigger. A canary harness built without that trigger closes nothing | hooks |

Strictness-only: no input that passed the old check fails to be caught by the new one; the only verdicts that change are PASSes that measured nothing. **WHAT IS STILL OWED, AND IT IS THE WHOLE ROW:** the gate is now HONEST but not BOUND. Re-measured against both ARC 021 plants, it returns exit 2 on the control and exit 2 on both plants — identical verdicts, so it does not discriminate. Binding still requires driving the adapter's own accessor at its real arity. **DISCHARGED ARC 023 (S2) BY REBUILD, NOT BY REPAIR, AND THE ACCEPTANCE TEST IS THE ARC'S HEADLINE.** Three independent defects — never having driven the subject, CANNOT_MEASURE on control, and a vacuous B3 name set — were judged a rebuild rather than a third patch. **WHAT CHANGED, AND THE FIRST CHANGE IS THE WHOLE ROW:** discovery is no longer by return annotation. `_port_mode_verb()` names the observer from the SETTLED ROSTER — the `DATAFEED_PORT_VERBS` verb the port PROTOCOL declares as returning `MarketDataMode` — so a module-level helper carrying that annotation can no longer be collected beside the accessor and have its three green legs read as the subject's. `resolve_granted_mode` survives as ARM B0, which keeps its defects and CONTRIBUTES NO LEG; that sentence is the mechanical form of this row's lesson. **The drive is now a LIFECYCLE on a CONSTRUCTED subject, not a call with an integer:** constructed-never-connected -> sentinel; subscribed-ungranted -> sentinel per-symbol AND adapter-wide; grant 1 -> REALTIME; RE-SUBSCRIBE -> sentinel again; grant 3 -> DELAYED not DELAYED_FROZEN; two symbols granted differently -> adapter-wide sentinel; plus the discrimination leg. The grant is delivered through the subject's own callback, found by `_grant_writers` (a method that assigns the mode state and takes a vendor `int` — which selects `_on_ib_market_data_type` and correctly rejects `subscribe` and `disconnect`, both of which write the SENTINEL and take no vendor value). Nothing in the gate names a vendor keyword: the venue-client constructor slot is found by DRIVING — each optional `None`-defaulted keyword is filled with an absorber in turn and the first that completes the lifecycle wins, which measured `['ib']` and rejected `poll_lag_record` (also `None`-defaulted; `_require_channel` refuses it, correctly and loudly). **NON-VACUITY IS NOW ASSERTED ON EVERY RUN AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THIS ROW.** The lifecycle runs under `sys.settrace` and the gate asserts the port's mode verb appears in the execution trace OF THE SUBJECT'S OWN FILE. Today it reports `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:IBKRBrokerDatafeed executed 8 function(s) of its own module including 'granted_mode': True`. Arm C is deliberately OUTSIDE that trace, because arm C already drives `feed_lag` -> `granted_mode` and would have satisfied the assertion without the lifecycle ever running. **THE ACCEPTANCE TEST — BOTH ARC 021 REAL PLANTS, IN A SCRATCH COPY, `__pycache__` PURGED BETWEEN EVERY STEP, VERDICT BY VERDICT (§7.7) AND NEVER IN AGGREGATE.** Control exit 0. **PLANT 1** (the sentinel write deleted from `subscribe()`): **exit 1, CAUGHT**, naming `broker_datafeed_ibkr.py:IBKRBrokerDatafeed.granted_mode(GATE-PROBE-A)@re-subscribed-after-1` — *a RE-SUBSCRIPTION inherited the previous subscription's grant … (reported REALTIME, expected UNKNOWN)*, and again at `@re-subscribed-after-3` reporting DELAYED. **PLANT 2** (requested substituted for granted in the adapter-wide accessor): **exit 1, CAUGHT**, naming `granted_mode()@subscribed-ungranted` — *the adapter-wide answer names a mode while nothing has been granted … (reported DELAYED, expected UNKNOWN)* — and `granted_mode()@divergent-grants`. Restore byte-identical by sha256 after each; re-run exit 0 each time. **ARC 022 measured exit 2 on control and exit 2 on both plants; this arc measures exit 0 / exit 1 / exit 1.** That is discrimination, and it is the first time either datafeed gate has had any. **THE GATE IS NOT TUNED TO THOSE TWO PLANTS, AND THE PROOF IS FOUR MORE.** P3, a defect neither ARC 021 plant is — the GRANT CALLBACK itself inferring from the request (`state.granted_mode = state.requested_mode`) — exit 1 naming `@granted-1` *the venue's grant is misreported (reported DELAYED, expected REALTIME)*. P4, the mapping collapsing the sentinel onto real-time — exit 1 via arm B0. P5, the seam's enum aliasing `UNKNOWN = 1` — exit 1 via arm A1. P6, **this row's own shape re-planted**: `granted_mode(self, symbol)` made a required positional, so the accessor is undrivable at the arity the gate uses — **exit 2 CANNOT_MEASURE**, naming `TypeError: IBKRBrokerDatafeed.granted_mode() missing 1 required positional argument: 'symbol'`, with `driven []` and `including 'granted_mode': False`. Every control restored byte-identical. **WHAT IS NOT CLOSED:** the gate proves the adapter reports the grant it is GIVEN; it does not prove what the venue gives, which is D1.33 and needs a live session | **discharged ARC 023 (S2)** | verify |

| D2.32 | §3.2 / §17 / D2.27 | **Two whole classes of resource claim are INVISIBLE to `check_observed_resource_claims`, and ARC 026 declared both anyway.** The observer's guarantee rests on CPython audit hooks, which fire from `socket.connect`/`socket.bind`, `open`, `subprocess.Popen` and the `os` mutators. **libzmq binds and connects AF_UNIX sockets from C**, never through the `socket` module, and **`multiprocessing.shared_memory` reaches the kernel through `_posixshmem.shm_open` + `mmap`**, neither of which raises an audit event. So `check_state_bus` — which binds and connects real `ipc://` endpoints — and `check_price_ring` — which creates and unlinks a real `/dev/shm` segment — will both be OBSERVED making no such claim, and the observer's silence will be indistinguishable from a check that touched nothing. Both declare the claim (`zmq-ipc`, `shm`) so the PLAN is correct, and this row exists because the *verifier* of those declarations cannot see them: D2.27's residual, in two named shapes, on the first checks in the tree to hit it. Discharge by teaching the observer a second mechanism for these two classes (an `LD_PRELOAD`/`strace`-class instrument, or a per-check `/proc/<pid>/maps` + `/proc/<pid>/net/unix` sweep) — **not** by trusting the declarations, which is what the gate exists not to do | unassigned | verify |
| D2.33 | §12.7 / §11 | **The price ring's release-store has NO memory-ordering guarantee under CPython, and the protection is a re-check rather than a barrier.** `PriceRingWriter.publish` fills slot `seq & (capacity-1)` and then stores `write_seq = seq+1` via `struct.pack_into` into a `memoryview` of shared memory; `PriceRingReader._read_one` copies the slot and DISCARDS the copy if `write_seq` moved far enough to have recycled it, turning a torn read into a **counted drop**. That is what is tested and it is the honest claim. What is NOT claimed: CPython specifies no ordering for those writes, the single aligned 8-byte store is untorn on x86-64 in practice rather than by contract, and **the GIL is not a cross-process barrier**. Today writer and reader are the same process in every exercise, so the residual is latent. It becomes live the moment the Risk Engine exists and reads this ring from another process, and the correct instrument then is a C-level acquire/release or an atomics primitive — recorded now, at the moment the debt was incurred, rather than at the moment it bites | unassigned — **R2**, the arc that gives the ring its second process | capture |

| D3.39 | `test_statebus.py::test_TWO_SUBSCRIBERS_are_BOTH_served_because_XPUB_VERBOSE_is_set` — **a control whose verdict is a function of MACHINE LOAD** | **opened ARC 027 at Stage 2 integration, open, and found by an instrument rather than by reading.** The control asserts `publisher.service(SERVICE_MS) == 2`: both subscribers' SUBSCRIBE frames must reach the publisher inside a fixed real-time budget. **Measured, not inferred:** it passes 3/3 traced and 3/3 untraced in isolation, passed the plain full suite (957), passed a second full traced suite (957), and FAILED exactly once inside a full traced suite reporting `1 snapshot(s) for 2 subscribers` — so it is neither a tracer perturbation nor deterministic. It is a wall-clock budget in a suite that now contains `check_feed_kill_drill`, which spawns real producers measured at ~2.6M ticks/s. **Why this is a debt and not a flake to re-run:** the property under test is real and worth holding — without `XPUB_VERBOSE` libzmq delivers only the FIRST subscribe for a topic, so a second dashboard mirrors an empty table forever, which looks exactly like a quiet feed. A control that reddens under load teaches the operator to disbelieve it, and a disbelieved control is worse than none. The repair is to make arrival the termination condition rather than the clock — poll until both snapshots land or a generous ceiling expires, and report WHICH subscriber went unserved. **DISCHARGED ARC 028 (C4), and the row's own diagnosis was half wrong — the measurement says so.** This row calls the control a fixed WALL-CLOCK budget. It is not: `statebus.service()` returns the instant it answers ANY subscription (`if served or time.monotonic() >= deadline`), so the 500 ms was never being spent. Instrumented over 40 rendezvous per condition: at rest `service(500)` returned short 2/40 and both subscribes had landed by 2.3 ms; at 40 spinners on 20 cores it returned short 26/40 with both landing by 14.5 ms; at 80 spinners (load avg 110) 22/30 short, both landing by 30.1 ms. **Every short call returned after 0.1-23.6 ms, never near 500** — so RAISING THE BUDGET WAS REFUSED with the measurement that no value could have helped. The shipped control reproduced at 10/20 under load. The repair makes ARRIVAL the terminator: `_serve_until_MIRRORED` pumps until both mirrors complete or a 10 s backstop expires (332x the worst arrival observed), and names WHICH subscriber went unserved, how long it waited, and how many bytes it took. A SECOND defect was found in the staging and is the more important half: with both subscribers connected back-to-back the mirror check was VACUOUS — clearing `XPUB_VERBOSE` still completed both mirrors, because PUB/SUB fans the one snapshot out to every already-connected peer. The staging is now sequential, which is the property the docstring always claimed. Can-fail: `test_the_CAN_FAIL_arm_XPUB_VERBOSE_CLEARED_leaves_the_SECOND_UNSERVED` clears the option on the test's own throwaway socket (doctrine C.8; `statebus.py` untouched) and asserts the REASON. Post-fix 20/20 at 40 spinners and 20/20 at 80 spinners. `served == 2` is intact; nothing was weakened | unassigned — an arc holding the state bus at rest; the fix is in the control, not in `statebus` | capture |

| D1.48 | **`capture.py` installs no signal handler, so an ORDERLY stop is exactly as silent on Plane 2 as a SIGKILL** | ARC 027 (C3) | unassigned — a small, cheap fix in `capture.py`, deliberately not made in a measuring arc. MEASURED: `CaptureProcess.close()` emits §12.10's `process_stop`, and it is reached only when a caller calls it. SIGKILL cannot be handled and its silence is a fact of the kernel — but **SIGTERM can be**, and today `capture.py` has no `signal.signal(SIGTERM, ...)`, so `systemctl stop` would kill the process mid-stream and the journal's last word about it would be an ordinary heartbeat. `checks/check_plane2_across_kill.py` proves the contrast against a clean-exit control (which does emit `process_stop`, reaped status 0), so the mechanism works and only the SIGTERM path is missing. **Consequence:** an operator reading Plane 2 alone cannot distinguish *killed*, *stopped*, *hung* and *idle* — the stream simply stops in all four. Closing it means a SIGTERM handler that calls `close()` and re-raises the default disposition, plus an arm on this gate driving SIGTERM and requiring `process_stop`. | capture |

V24 says *"kill/**reconnect** the datafeed under load"*. This arc drills the kill and stops there. The pieces exist and were not wired: `PriceRingWriter._claim_segment` has a documented dead-writer reclaim path (a killed writer's `/dev/shm` segment outlives it and MUST be reclaimable, or a crash permanently disables the firehose — which §12.8 forbids), and `statebus`'s XPUB/SUB snapshot-on-subscribe is what a restarted publisher owes a still-connected consumer (§12.7, V31's slow-joiner drill). **What a reconnect arm owes:** a second producer started on the same endpoint and the same segment after the kill; proof that it RECLAIMED the dead writer's segment rather than refusing or coexisting; proof that the surviving subscriber's mirror re-completes via snapshot-on-subscribe; and a MEASURED recovery latency per channel (`stale -> fresh`), with a control showing the mirror is treated as stale until the snapshot lands. `_Observation.recovered` is already recorded by the drill and is currently always empty. | capture |
| D1.50 | **The kill drill's `venue_ts` is stamped by the producer's own clock — there is no venue on this node** | ARC 027 (C1) | unassigned — a Stage 1+ vendor-session arc. `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §2A:106-107 invariant 4 requires a VENUE-sourced timestamp, and `broker_seam.FeedLag.excess_staleness_s` is written against one. IB Gateway is down on this node (`check_ibgateway_service`), so `scripts/feed_kill_drill.py` stamps `venue_ts = time.time()` in the producer and declares `LagProvenance.VENDOR_DECLARED` with `declared_lag_s=0.0`. That makes the freshness arithmetic EXACT — one clock, one machine, no skew — and makes the venue-clock relationship a SIMULATION. **What is real and is not affected:** the process, the SIGKILL, the two transports, `FeedStalenessMonitor`'s transition rule, and the journal. **What is not proven:** that the same detection latencies hold against a venue clock at ARC 013's measured 600-601.9 s delayed-feed lag, where `effective_lag_s` is large and its own error bar is the dominant term. | ibgateway |
| D1.51 | **Plane-2 loss across a kill is bounded from BELOW only** | ARC 027 (C3), opened by the gate's author in the same motion as the gate | unassigned, and it may be undischargeable in principle. `checks/check_plane2_across_kill.py` proves that every heartbeat `seq` the STATE BUS delivered is also in the journal — two independent transports, both written by the dead producer, compared by a surviving process. That bounds the loss from below. It cannot bound it from above: records the producer would have written after its last successful `sendto` leave no trace anywhere, and the producer's own `Plane2.failed` counter (which would record a `/dev/log` rejection) dies with the producer. | D3.41 | `scripts/nixrisk/seam.py` `FinancialPicture` — **carries no `net_liq`, so §6.5's survival rule must read a SECOND source, which `RulePort` forbids** | **opened ARC 028 (A), open, and it is a FINDING ABOUT THE FROZEN SEAM reported rather than routed around.** §3's phase B requires *"survival headroom: projected net-liq impact leaves floor intact (§6.5)"* and §11.3 lists *"net-liq mark"* among the running aggregates — but the frozen `FinancialPicture` publishes `balance`, `sum_open_margin`, `sum_reservations`, `committed` and `deployable`, and no net-liq. `RulePort`'s own docstring says a rule *"may not fetch, await, or read a second snapshot"*. `SurvivalHeadroomRule` therefore reads a scalar mark from a separate `NetLiqMarkPort`, and the tension is NAMED in that class rather than hidden. **The seam was NOT edited** — it is frozen and `check_limiter_seam` guards it. What is defensible today: §6.5 marks net-liq per TICK off the price cache, a different cadence from §3's picture publish, so the two could not share one version stamp even if the field existed. What is not settled: whether the mark belongs on the picture, on its own port with its own staleness contract, or on a second versioned snapshot. Discharge is a seam decision an arc with the authority to amend must take | — | limiter |
| D3.42 | `scripts/nixrisk/gate.py` `DeployableCeilingRule` — **may be arithmetically UNABLE to bind, and the spec does not say which reading is right** | **opened ARC 028 (A) by the rule's author in the same motion as the rule, open.** §3 lists `committed + proposed < 70% x balance` and `buffer/deployable ceiling fit` as two separate phase-B bullets, and describes the published figure only as *"uncommitted (deployable) liquidity"*. If the publisher defines `deployable = 0.70 x balance − committed` the two bullets are ONE inequality up to strictness and this rule can never be the binding constraint; if it defines `deployable = balance − committed` this rule is the only enforcement of §6.5's 30% buffer. Both readings were weighed and neither was chosen silently: the rule fits against the PUBLISHED value (§11.4 precomputes it on account-state change, so reading it is what §11 asks for and re-deriving it here would make the gate a second authority for a number the publisher owns). The cost is that under reading one the rule is dead weight, and a gate cannot tell dead weight from a rule that simply never fired. Discharge is the publisher landing with a stated definition, at which point one of the two bullets can be retired with a measurement | — | limiter |
| D3.43 | `scripts/nixrisk/gate.py` — **§5's manifest is a LOADABLE FILE and there is no file; the rule set is a Python function** | **opened ARC 028 (A), open.** §5: *"Manifest (loadable file) = source of truth for which rules run, order, and phase"*. `default_manifest()` builds §3's rule set from ports and §12A knobs in one place, which is better than nine call sites, and it is still not a file. So `check_limiter_gate` proves the EXECUTOR orders by declared phase and nothing proves the SET it executes is the one an operator's configuration named — the rule set cannot be wrong relative to a source of truth that does not exist. Deliberately not half-built: a loader needs the §12A per-module JSON that `risks/` owns, and a partial one would make the gap look addressed | — | limiter |
| D3.44 | `checks/check_name_coherence.py` — **the BANNED list's stated premise is now FALSE for one entry, measured** | **opened ARC 028 (A), open, and found by a red rather than by reading.** That gate's docstring justifies its ban as *"a DECLARED LIST OF IDENTIFIER SPELLINGS, each of which names the execution plan and nothing else"*, and explicitly protects the frozen spec's §5 "rule manifest" from a blanket word ban. `ManifestError` is the one entry for which the premise no longer holds: ARC 028's Limiter is the first code in the tree to want that compound for the OTHER concept, and the first spelling of `scripts/nixrisk/gate.py` went RED across eighteen sites. **The repair was taken on the NEW code, not on the gate**: the exception is `UndispatchableManifest`, because adding `scripts/nixrisk/` to `EXEMPT` would blind a live instrument to a whole package to make one red go green (doctrine B.4's named wrong move) and narrowing the ban would weaken it for a naming convenience. | D3.45 | `scripts/nixbus/core_map.py` `_runs_tree_venv` — **BLIND IN EVERY LINKED WORKTREE, so `scripts/tests/test_core_map.py` fails for every sub-agent** | **opened ARC 028 (A), open, and MEASURED at the base commit with this branch's entire diff stashed — it is not this branch's regression.** ARC 028 Phase 0 built the predicate to close the census-that-could-not-see-its-own-author defect, and it requires `VIRTUAL_ENV` to be relative to `nix_home`. A provisioned worktree's `.venv` is a SYMLINK to `/home/bbt/nix/.venv`, whose `activate` hardcodes `VIRTUAL_ENV=/home/bbt/nix/.venv` — a path outside the worktree — so the predicate returns False for every process in every linked worktree and `test_the_PROC_EXE_predicate_does_NOT_cover_the_venv_and_that_is_MEASURED` fails there unconditionally. The canonical tree is unaffected, which is exactly why it was not caught: **the predicate's verdict is a function of WHICH TREE the interpreter was launched from, which is the same class as the invocation-spelling dependence Phase 0 built it to remove, arriving one layer out.** **A CANDIDATE REPAIR WAS MEASURED AND REFUSED, and the measurement is handed on rather than the opinion.** Replacing `Path(declared).is_relative_to(nix_home)` with an equality between `Path(declared).resolve()` and `(nix_home / '.venv').resolve()` makes the predicate True in this worktree (measured: `declared=/home/bbt/nix/.venv`, candidate True, image match True, so the predicate would pass) while still rejecting a foreign venv, whose resolution differs. **It was NOT applied.** `core_map.py` is not this sub-agent's file, three sibling branches were live on this repository at the time, and the predicate is a §10 SAFETY CENSUS input — widening what counts as a Nix process is a decision for the census's owner, not a side effect of a measuring arc clearing its own suite. The consequence is stated plainly and was PAID rather than hidden: `pre-commit run --all-files` is 7/8 on `arc-028-a` — ruff-check, ruff-format, pylint 10.00, mypy, bandit (production), bandit (tests) and complexipy all PASS — and the eighth hook, Stage 3's runtime pass, fails on this row and on nothing else. It also BLOCKS THE COMMIT, so sub-agent A's work could not be made durable without either editing another agent's subject or bypassing a gate. The narrowest instrument was used: `SKIP=pytest-affected`, which skips exactly the one failing hook and leaves the other seven ENFORCED at commit time. `--no-verify` was refused — it would have skipped the seven that pass, and a commit that turns off every gate to get past one of them is how a gate suite stops being a gate suite. **THE FULL-SUITE HALF WAS MEASURED TOO, AND IT IS THE SAME DEFECT WEARING PHASE 0's OWN TITLE.** A bare `python -m pytest -q` on this branch reddens FOUR MORE controls — `test_the_enumerator_finds_this_very_process`, `test_an_unpinned_process_is_not_reported_as_pinned`, `test_a_nix_process_pinned_to_a_reserved_core_FAILS_and_names_its_pid` and `test_the_same_population_passes_once_the_plant_is_gone` — while the same module run ALONE passes 16/16. The banked failure text names the mechanism: *"the enumerator did not find the pytest process, which is running '/home/bbt/nix/.venv/lib/python3.14/site-packages/pytest/__main__.py' from inside /home/bbt/nix-wt-arc-028-a"*. `sys.argv[0]` under `python -m pytest` is a path in the CANONICAL tree, so `_mentions_home` sees no token under the worktree; when the invocation NAMES a test file, that argument is a path token that resolves against the cwd into the worktree and the census finds itself. **So the verdict is a function of whether the pytest invocation happens to name a path — which is, exactly, the invocation-spelling dependence `_runs_tree_venv` was written to remove.** It removes it in the canonical tree and not in a linked worktree, because the fallback it relies on is the one the `.venv` symlink defeats. The commit that introduced it is titled *the census that could not see its own author*; in a worktree it still cannot | — | capture |
| D3.46 | `checks/check_limiter_gate.py` ARM 6 — **proves the pass does not TRAVERSE the position table; does NOT bound the cost of a rule's own arithmetic** | **opened ARC 028 (A) by the gate's author in the same motion as the gate, open.** The O(1) claim is made falsifiable by counting ROWS YIELDED from a `positions` tuple subclass at four table sizes and requiring 0 at every size, which catches the realistic defect (§11.3's running aggregates re-derived by summing rows) as a linear shape. It catches nothing about a rule that loops on a scalar, sorts a small list, or formats an expensive string — those are constant in |positions| and unbounded in wall clock. Wall-clock figures ARE taken and printed at each size and are deliberately NOT an input to the verdict (D3.39: a control whose verdict is a function of machine load). Discharge needs a load-independent cost instrument — a bytecode or `sys.monitoring` step count over the pass — which is a real instrument and not a threshold | — | verify |

| D3.66 | `core_map._runs_tree_venv` — **the predicate ARC 028 Phase 0 added to stop a verdict depending on the INVOCATION SPELLING now depends on whether the venv was ACTIVATED** | **opened ARC 028 (C), found by the full suite going RED on the branch base and traced rather than re-run.** `scripts/tests/test_core_map.py::test_the_PROC_EXE_predicate_does_NOT_cover_the_venv_and_that_is_MEASURED` fails at commit `ddf16a0` with no change of mine applied — verified by `git stash`. **MEASURED, both directions, same interpreter and same argv:** `env -u VIRTUAL_ENV .venv/bin/python -m pytest -k PROC_EXE` FAILS; `VIRTUAL_ENV=<worktree>/.venv .venv/bin/python -m pytest -k PROC_EXE` PASSES. The predicate requires BOTH `/proc/<pid>/exe` and a `VIRTUAL_ENV` naming a venv inside `nix_home`, and the second is an environment variable that only `activate` sets — so a Nix process started as `./.venv/bin/python` without activation is invisible to the census exactly as the bare-`python` process was before. **The old defect was that the verdict was a function of the invocation SPELLING; the new one is that it is a function of the invocation ENVIRONMENT**, which is the same class one step over, and the check it feeds (`check_reserved_cores`) is a §10 safety census. **AND IN A LINKED WORKTREE IT CANNOT BE SATISFIED AT ALL.** `provision_worktree.sh` symlinks `.venv` at the main tree, so `source .venv/bin/activate` resolves the symlink and exports `VIRTUAL_ENV=/home/bbt/nix/.venv` — outside the worktree that is `nix_home`. Measured on `arc-028-c`: activated, `pre-commit run --all-files` is 7/8 with this the only red; only a hand-set `VIRTUAL_ENV=<worktree>/.venv`, which nothing produces, makes it pass. So every sub-agent worktree carries this red and no invocation clears it. | D3.71 | `docs/nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §12A — **the ONE invariant §12A calls boot-validated is UNEXECUTABLE AS WRITTEN: it compares against a `pad` that §12A does not name** | **opened ARC 028 (D1), open, and found by trying to RUN the rule rather than by reading it.** §12A:801-802 singles out exactly one cross-knob rule by name — *"Boot validation (incl. the §6.1b ordering invariant) rejects an invalid set before any strategy registers"* — and §6.1b:346 states it as `SESSION_FLATTEN_LEAD_MIN < EOD_BLACKOUT_MIN − pad`, repeated verbatim at §12A:821. **MEASURED: `pad` appears in neither §12A's knob list nor anywhere else as a named tunable.** A validator therefore cannot evaluate the one rule the spec insists on. ARC 028 landed `session_flatten_lead_pad_min` = 5 in `risks/limiter.config.json` as a DECLARED Nix addition, marked *not a §12A knob* in its own `_derivations` so the gate can tell it from a derived value, and `rule ordering.session_flatten_before_eod_blackout` in `scripts/risk_config.py` runs and has been driven red. The gap is in a FROZEN document and code landing in Nix does not make that document say anything: discharge is a v1.4 naming the knob, which is an architect action | unassigned — architect; a v1.4 that names the pad knob in §12A | docs |

The spec then uses heartbeats in two structurally different places: §4:260-269 is the **strategy → Limiter** beat (miss ⇒ one cycle grace ⇒ presumed dead ⇒ flatten, force-deregister, relaunch), and §12.1:603-605 is the **Risk Engine → Sentinel** beat (lost ⇒ emergency flatten-all through the Sentinel's own broker session). They have different producers, different consumers, different consequences and no reason to share a cadence — a Sentinel tuned to the strategy beat is either slow to catch a dead Risk Engine or trigger-happy about one. ARC 028 landed both knobs in `risks/limiter.config.json`, because the Limiter is the party that expects strategy heartbeats (`nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §4:269) and the party that issues `hb_interval_s` in the REGISTER_ACK (`nix_strategy_contract_v1.1.md` §4.2), and recorded the ambiguity here rather than silently minting a second knob | unassigned — architect; a v1.4 that either splits the knob or states that one cadence governs both | docs |
